\displaystyle+(A_{dd}-\bar{A}_{dd}-A_{cc}+\bar{A}_{cc}-\mathcal{A}_{d}+\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{d}+\mathcal{A}_{c}-\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{c})z_{a}^{2}z_{c}z_{d}+(A_{aa}-\bar{A}_{aa}-A_{cc}+\bar{A}_{cc}-\mathcal{A}_{a}+\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{a}+\mathcal{A}_{c}-\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{c})z_{a}z_{b}^{2}z_{c}

\displaystyle+(A_{dd}-\bar{A}_{dd}-A_{aa}+\bar{A}_{aa}-\mathcal{A}_{d}+\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{d}+\mathcal{A}_{a}-\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{a})z_{a}z_{b}^{2}z_{d}+(A_{bb}-\bar{A}_{bb}-A_{aa}+\bar{A}_{aa}-\mathcal{A}_{b}+\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{b}+\mathcal{A}_{a}-\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{a})z_{a}z_{b}z_{c}^{2}

\displaystyle+(A_{aa}-\bar{A}_{aa}-A_{bb}+\bar{A}_{bb}-\mathcal{A}_{a}+\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{a}+\mathcal{A}_{b}-\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{b})z_{a}z_{b}z_{d}^{2}+(A_{aa}-\bar{A}_{aa}-A_{dd}+\bar{A}_{dd}-\mathcal{A}_{a}+\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{a}+\mathcal{A}_{d}-\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{d})z_{a}z_{c}^{2}z_{d}

\displaystyle+(A_{cc}-\bar{A}_{cc}-A_{aa}+\bar{A}_{aa}-\mathcal{A}_{c}+\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{c}+\mathcal{A}_{a}-\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{a})z_{a}z_{c}z_{d}^{2}+(A_{cc}-\bar{A}_{cc}-A_{dd}+\bar{A}_{dd}-\mathcal{A}_{c}+\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{c}+\mathcal{A}_{d}-\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{d})z_{b}^{2}z_{c}z_{d}

\displaystyle+(A_{dd}-\bar{A}_{dd}-A_{bb}+\bar{A}_{bb}-\mathcal{A}_{d}+\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{d}+\mathcal{A}_{b}-\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{b})z_{b}z_{c}^{2}z_{d}+(A_{bb}-\bar{A}_{bb}-A_{cc}+\bar{A}_{cc}-\mathcal{A}_{b}+\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{b}+\mathcal{A}_{c}-\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{c})z_{b}z_{c}z_{d}^{2}\Big]\,,

which is equivalent to

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}[c_{abcd}](\boldsymbol{z})=2i\gamma_{abcd}(\boldsymbol{z})\Big[

\displaystyle(\Imag(A_{cc}-A_{bb})-\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{c}-\mathcal{A}_{b}))z_{a}^{2}z_{b}z_{c}+(\Imag(A_{bb}-A_{dd})-\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{b}-\mathcal{A}_{d}))z_{a}^{2}z_{b}z_{d}

\displaystyle+(\Imag(A_{dd}-A_{cc})-\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{d}-\mathcal{A}_{c}))z_{a}^{2}z_{c}z_{d}+(\Imag(A_{aa}-A_{cc})-\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{a}-\mathcal{A}_{c}))z_{a}z_{b}^{2}z_{c}

\displaystyle+(\Imag(A_{dd}-A_{aa})-\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{d}-\mathcal{A}_{a}))z_{a}z_{b}^{2}z_{d}+(\Imag(A_{bb}-A_{aa})-\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{b}-\mathcal{A}_{a}))z_{a}z_{b}z_{c}^{2}

\displaystyle+(\Imag(A_{aa}-A_{bb})-\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{a}-\mathcal{A}_{b}))z_{a}z_{b}z_{d}^{2}+(\Imag(A_{aa}-A_{dd})-\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{a}-\mathcal{A}_{d}))z_{a}z_{c}^{2}z_{d}

\displaystyle+(\Imag(A_{cc}-A_{aa})-\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{c}-\mathcal{A}_{a}))z_{a}z_{c}z_{d}^{2}+(\Imag(A_{cc}-A_{dd})-\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{c}-\mathcal{A}_{d}))z_{b}^{2}z_{c}z_{d}

\displaystyle+(\Imag(A_{dd}-A_{bb})-\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{d}-\mathcal{A}_{b}))z_{b}z_{c}^{2}z_{d}+(\Imag(A_{bb}-A_{cc})-\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{b}-\mathcal{A}_{c}))z_{b}z_{c}z_{d}^{2}\Big]\,.

Since
\Imag(A_{jj})=\omega_{j}/2
, one gets

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}[c_{abcd}](\boldsymbol{z})=i\gamma_{abcd}(\boldsymbol{z})\Big[

\displaystyle((\omega_{c}-\omega_{b})-2\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{c}-\mathcal{A}_{b}))z_{a}^{2}z_{b}z_{c}+((\omega_{b}-\omega_{d})-2\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{b}-\mathcal{A}_{d}))z_{a}^{2}z_{b}z_{d}

\displaystyle+((\omega_{d}-\omega_{c})-2\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{d}-\mathcal{A}_{c}))z_{a}^{2}z_{c}z_{d}+((\omega_{a}-\omega_{c})-2\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{a}-\mathcal{A}_{c}))z_{a}z_{b}^{2}z_{c}

\displaystyle+((\omega_{d}-\omega_{a})-2\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{d}-\mathcal{A}_{a}))z_{a}z_{b}^{2}z_{d}+((\omega_{b}-\omega_{a})-2\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{b}-\mathcal{A}_{a}))z_{a}z_{b}z_{c}^{2}

\displaystyle+((\omega_{a}-\omega_{b})-2\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{a}-\mathcal{A}_{b}))z_{a}z_{b}z_{d}^{2}+((\omega_{a}-\omega_{d})-2\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{a}-\mathcal{A}_{d}))z_{a}z_{c}^{2}z_{d}

\displaystyle+((\omega_{c}-\omega_{a})-2\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{c}-\mathcal{A}_{a}))z_{a}z_{c}z_{d}^{2}+((\omega_{c}-\omega_{d})-2\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{c}-\mathcal{A}_{d}))z_{b}^{2}z_{c}z_{d}

\displaystyle+((\omega_{d}-\omega_{b})-2\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{d}-\mathcal{A}_{b}))z_{b}z_{c}^{2}z_{d}+((\omega_{b}-\omega_{c})-2\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{b}-\mathcal{A}_{c}))z_{b}z_{c}z_{d}^{2}\Big]\,.

Using Eq. (
S67
) (condition 3) makes each term fall, yielding
\mathcal{K}[c_{abcd}](\boldsymbol{z})=0
and the sufficiency of the three conditions of the theorem.

(
\Rightarrow
) For the necessary conditions, we have to solve
\mathcal{K}[c_{abcd}](\boldsymbol{z})=0
in terms of
A_{ak},A_{bk},A_{ck},A_{dk}
for all
k\in\{1,...,N\}
. The monomials resulting from the last summation on
k\notin\{a,b,c,d\}
in Eq. (
S73
) are all independent from the other terms of the expression, which only depend on
z_{a},z_{b},z_{c},z_{d}
. They can thus be treated separately, i.e., such that

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\textstyle{\sum_{k\in\{a,b,c,d\}}}\Big[(A_{ck}-A_{ak})(z_{d}-z_{b})(z_{c}-z_{b})(z_{d}-z_{a})z_{k}+(A_{dk}-A_{bk})(z_{c}-z_{a})(z_{c}-z_{b})(z_{d}-z_{a})z_{k}

\displaystyle+(A_{bk}-A_{ck})(z_{c}-z_{a})(z_{d}-z_{b})(z_{d}-z_{a})z_{k}+(A_{ak}-A_{dk})(z_{c}-z_{a})(z_{d}-z_{b})(z_{c}-z_{b})z_{k}

\displaystyle-(\bar{A}_{ak}-\bar{A}_{bk})z_{a}^{2}z_{b}^{2}(z_{c}-z_{d})\bar{z}_{k}-(\bar{A}_{ck}-\bar{A}_{ak})z_{a}^{2}z_{c}^{2}(z_{b}-z_{d})\bar{z}_{k}-(\bar{A}_{ak}-\bar{A}_{dk})z_{a}^{2}z_{d}^{2}(z_{b}-z_{c})\bar{z}_{k}

\displaystyle-(\bar{A}_{bk}-\bar{A}_{ck})z_{b}^{2}z_{c}^{2}(z_{a}-z_{d})\bar{z}_{k}-(\bar{A}_{dk}-\bar{A}_{bk})z_{b}^{2}z_{d}^{2}(z_{a}-z_{c})\bar{z}_{k}-(\bar{A}_{ck}-\bar{A}_{dk})z_{c}^{2}z_{d}^{2}(z_{a}-z_{b})\bar{z}_{k}\Big]

(S74)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\textstyle{\sum_{k\notin\{a,b,c,d\}}}\Big[(A_{ck}-A_{ak})(z_{d}-z_{b})(z_{c}-z_{b})(z_{d}-z_{a})z_{k}+(A_{dk}-A_{bk})(z_{c}-z_{a})(z_{c}-z_{b})(z_{d}-z_{a})z_{k}

\displaystyle+(A_{bk}-A_{ck})(z_{c}-z_{a})(z_{d}-z_{b})(z_{d}-z_{a})z_{k}+(A_{ak}-A_{dk})(z_{c}-z_{a})(z_{d}-z_{b})(z_{c}-z_{b})z_{k}

\displaystyle-(\bar{A}_{ak}-\bar{A}_{bk})z_{a}^{2}z_{b}^{2}(z_{c}-z_{d})\bar{z}_{k}-(\bar{A}_{ck}-\bar{A}_{ak})z_{a}^{2}z_{c}^{2}(z_{b}-z_{d})\bar{z}_{k}-(\bar{A}_{ak}-\bar{A}_{dk})z_{a}^{2}z_{d}^{2}(z_{b}-z_{c})\bar{z}_{k}

\displaystyle-(\bar{A}_{bk}-\bar{A}_{ck})z_{b}^{2}z_{c}^{2}(z_{a}-z_{d})\bar{z}_{k}-(\bar{A}_{dk}-\bar{A}_{bk})z_{b}^{2}z_{d}^{2}(z_{a}-z_{c})\bar{z}_{k}-(\bar{A}_{ck}-\bar{A}_{dk})z_{c}^{2}z_{d}^{2}(z_{a}-z_{b})\bar{z}_{k}\Big]\,,

(S75)

where we have multiplied each equation by
\gamma_{abcd}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{z})=(z_{c}-z_{b})^{2}(z_{d}-z_{a})^{2}
.

On the one hand, the expanded form of the summand in Eq. (
S75
) for a given
k
contains 24 independent monomials:

\displaystyle\phantom{+}((A_{dk}-A_{ak})z_{b}z_{c}^{2}z_{k}^{2}+(A_{ak}-A_{ck})z_{b}z_{d}^{2}z_{k}^{2}+(A_{ak}-A_{bk})z_{c}^{2}z_{d}z_{k}^{2}+(A_{bk}-A_{ak})z_{c}z_{d}^{2}z_{k}^{2}

\displaystyle+(A_{ck}-A_{dk})z_{a}^{2}z_{b}z_{k}^{2}+(A_{dk}-A_{bk})z_{a}^{2}z_{c}z_{k}^{2}+(A_{bk}-A_{ck})z_{a}^{2}z_{d}z_{k}^{2}+(A_{dk}-A_{ck})z_{a}z_{b}^{2}z_{k}^{2}

\displaystyle+(A_{bk}-A_{dk})z_{a}z_{c}^{2}z_{k}^{2}+(A_{ak}-A_{dk})z_{b}^{2}z_{c}z_{k}^{2}+(A_{ck}-A_{ak})z_{b}^{2}z_{d}z_{k}^{2}+(A_{ck}-A_{bk})z_{a}z_{d}^{2}z_{k}^{2}

\displaystyle+(\bar{A}_{dk}-\bar{A}_{bk})z_{b}^{2}z_{c}z_{d}^{2}+(\bar{A}_{dk}-\bar{A}_{ck})z_{a}z_{c}^{2}z_{d}^{2}+(\bar{A}_{ck}-\bar{A}_{dk})z_{b}z_{c}^{2}z_{d}^{2}+(\bar{A}_{bk}-\bar{A}_{ck})z_{b}^{2}z_{c}^{2}z_{d}

\displaystyle+(\bar{A}_{ck}-\bar{A}_{ak})z_{a}^{2}z_{c}^{2}z_{d}+(\bar{A}_{ak}-\bar{A}_{dk})z_{a}^{2}z_{c}z_{d}^{2}+(\bar{A}_{ck}-\bar{A}_{bk})z_{a}z_{b}^{2}z_{c}^{2}+(\bar{A}_{bk}-\bar{A}_{dk})z_{a}z_{b}^{2}z_{d}^{2}

\displaystyle+(\bar{A}_{bk}-\bar{A}_{ak})z_{a}^{2}z_{b}^{2}z_{c}+(\bar{A}_{ak}-\bar{A}_{bk})z_{a}^{2}z_{b}^{2}z_{d}+(\bar{A}_{ak}-\bar{A}_{ck})z_{a}^{2}z_{b}z_{c}^{2}+(\bar{A}_{dk}-\bar{A}_{ak})z_{a}^{2}z_{b}z_{d}^{2})/z_{k}\,.

Since
k\notin\{a,b,c,d\}
, there are thus
N-4
groups of 24 monomials. The groups are all independent from one to another, because every group has a unique monomial depending on the group index
k\notin\{a,b,c,d\}
. The
24(N-4)
coefficients in front of the monomials must thus be zero to satisfy Eq. (
S75
) since
z_{x}^{u}z_{y}^{v}z_{z}^{w}
with
u,v,w\in\{1,2\}
and
1/z_{k}
are not zero. There are 12 coefficients having the form
A_{xk}-A_{yk}
and they must vanish (i.e.,
A_{xk}=A_{yk}
) for all pairs of indices
(x,y)
with
x,y\in\{a,b,c,d\}
and
k\notin\{a,b,c,d\}
. This readily implies
\bar{A}_{xk}=\bar{A}_{yk}
, meaning that the 12 other conditions with the form
\bar{A}_{xk}-\bar{A}_{yk}=0
are redundant. Therefore, the condition

A_{ak}=A_{bk}=A_{ck}=A_{dk},\quad\forall k\in\{1,...,N\}\setminus\{a,b,c,d\}\,,

i.e., Eq. (
S66
) of the second condition is necessary.

On the other hand, expanding Eq. (
S74
) yields an equation with 42 independent monomials:

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=((A_{ca}-A_{da})z_{a}^{4}z_{b}^{2}z_{c}z_{d}+(A_{da}-A_{ba})z_{a}^{4}z_{b}z_{c}^{2}z_{d}+(A_{ba}-A_{ca})z_{a}^{4}z_{b}z_{c}z_{d}^{2}+(\bar{A}_{bd}-\bar{A}_{ad})z_{a}^{3}z_{b}^{3}z_{c}^{2}

\displaystyle+(A_{cb}-A_{ca}+A_{da}-A_{db}-\bar{A}_{ac}+\bar{A}_{ad}+\bar{A}_{bc}-\bar{A}_{bd})z_{a}^{3}z_{b}^{3}z_{c}z_{d}+(\bar{A}_{ac}-\bar{A}_{bc})z_{a}^{3}z_{b}^{3}z_{d}^{2}

\displaystyle+(\bar{A}_{ad}-\bar{A}_{cd})z_{a}^{3}z_{b}^{2}z_{c}^{3}+(A_{cc}-A_{bb}+A_{db}-A_{dc}-\bar{A}_{ab}+\bar{A}_{ac}+\bar{A}_{bb}-\bar{A}_{cc})z_{a}^{3}z_{b}^{2}z_{c}^{2}z_{d}

\displaystyle+(A_{bb}-A_{cb}+A_{cd}-A_{dd}+\bar{A}_{ab}-\bar{A}_{bb}-\bar{A}_{ad}+\bar{A}_{dd})z_{a}^{3}z_{b}^{2}z_{c}z_{d}^{2}+(\bar{A}_{dc}-\bar{A}_{ac})z_{a}^{3}z_{b}^{2}z_{d}^{3}

\displaystyle+(A_{ba}-A_{bc}-A_{da}+A_{dc}+\bar{A}_{ab}-\bar{A}_{ad}-\bar{A}_{cb}+\bar{A}_{cd})z_{a}^{3}z_{b}z_{c}^{3}z_{d}+(A_{db}-A_{cb})z_{a}^{2}z_{b}^{4}z_{c}z_{d}

\displaystyle+(A_{bc}-A_{bd}-A_{cc}+A_{dd}-\bar{A}_{ac}+\bar{A}_{ad}+\bar{A}_{cc}-\bar{A}_{dd})z_{a}^{3}z_{b}z_{c}^{2}z_{d}^{2}+(\bar{A}_{cb}-\bar{A}_{ab})z_{a}^{3}z_{c}^{3}z_{d}^{2}

\displaystyle+(A_{bd}-A_{ba}+A_{ca}-A_{cd}-\bar{A}_{ab}+\bar{A}_{ac}+\bar{A}_{db}-\bar{A}_{dc})z_{a}^{3}z_{b}z_{c}z_{d}^{3}+(\bar{A}_{ab}-\bar{A}_{db})z_{a}^{3}z_{c}^{2}z_{d}^{3}

\displaystyle+(\bar{A}_{cd}-\bar{A}_{bd})z_{a}^{2}z_{b}^{3}z_{c}^{3}+(A_{aa}-A_{cc}-A_{da}+A_{dc}-\bar{A}_{aa}+\bar{A}_{ba}-\bar{A}_{bc}+\bar{A}_{cc})z_{a}^{2}z_{b}^{3}z_{c}^{2}z_{d}

\displaystyle+(A_{ca}-A_{aa}-A_{cd}+A_{dd}+\bar{A}_{aa}-\bar{A}_{ba}+\bar{A}_{bd}-\bar{A}_{dd})z_{a}^{2}z_{b}^{3}z_{c}z_{d}^{2}+(\bar{A}_{bc}-\bar{A}_{dc})z_{a}^{2}z_{b}^{3}z_{d}^{3}

\displaystyle+(A_{bb}-A_{aa}+A_{da}-A_{db}+\bar{A}_{aa}-\bar{A}_{bb}-\bar{A}_{ca}+\bar{A}_{cb})z_{a}^{2}z_{b}^{2}z_{c}^{3}z_{d}+(A_{ab}-A_{db})z_{a}z_{b}^{4}z_{c}^{2}z_{d}

\displaystyle+(A_{aa}-A_{bb}-A_{ca}+A_{cb}-\bar{A}_{aa}+\bar{A}_{bb}+\bar{A}_{da}-\bar{A}_{db})z_{a}^{2}z_{b}^{2}z_{c}z_{d}^{3}+(A_{bc}-A_{dc})z_{a}^{2}z_{b}z_{c}^{4}z_{d}

\displaystyle+(A_{aa}-A_{ba}+A_{bd}-A_{dd}-\bar{A}_{aa}+\bar{A}_{ca}-\bar{A}_{cd}+\bar{A}_{dd}+i\omega_{a}-i\omega_{d})z_{a}^{2}z_{b}z_{c}^{3}z_{d}^{2}+(\bar{A}_{db}-\bar{A}_{cb})z_{a}^{2}z_{c}^{3}z_{d}^{3}

\displaystyle+(A_{ba}-A_{aa}-A_{bc}+A_{cc}+\bar{A}_{aa}-\bar{A}_{da}-\bar{A}_{cc}+\bar{A}_{dc})z_{a}^{2}z_{b}z_{c}^{2}z_{d}^{3}+(A_{cd}-A_{bd})z_{a}^{2}z_{b}z_{c}z_{d}^{4}

\displaystyle+(A_{cb}-A_{ab})z_{a}z_{b}^{4}z_{c}z_{d}^{2}+(A_{ac}-A_{ab}+A_{db}-A_{dc}-\bar{A}_{ba}+\bar{A}_{ca}+\bar{A}_{bd}-\bar{A}_{cd})z_{a}z_{b}^{3}z_{c}^{3}z_{d}

\displaystyle+(A_{ad}-A_{ac}+A_{cc}-A_{dd}+\bar{A}_{bc}-\bar{A}_{bd}-\bar{A}_{cc}+\bar{A}_{dd})z_{a}z_{b}^{3}z_{c}^{2}z_{d}^{2}+(A_{ac}-A_{bc})z_{a}z_{b}z_{c}^{4}z_{d}^{2}

\displaystyle+(A_{ab}-A_{ad}-A_{cb}+A_{cd}+\bar{A}_{ba}-\bar{A}_{bc}-\bar{A}_{da}+\bar{A}_{dc})z_{a}z_{b}^{3}z_{c}z_{d}^{3}+(A_{dc}-A_{ac})z_{a}z_{b}^{2}z_{c}^{4}z_{d}

\displaystyle+(A_{ab}-A_{ad}-A_{bb}+A_{dd}+\bar{A}_{bb}-\bar{A}_{cb}+\bar{A}_{cd}-\bar{A}_{dd})z_{a}z_{b}^{2}z_{c}^{3}z_{d}^{2}+(A_{ad}-A_{cd})z_{a}z_{b}^{2}z_{c}z_{d}^{4}

\displaystyle+(A_{ac}-A_{ab}+A_{bb}-A_{cc}-\bar{A}_{bb}+\bar{A}_{cc}+\bar{A}_{db}-\bar{A}_{dc})z_{a}z_{b}^{2}z_{c}^{2}z_{d}^{3}+(A_{bd}-A_{ad})z_{a}z_{b}z_{c}^{2}z_{d}^{4}

\displaystyle+(A_{ad}-A_{ac}+A_{bc}-A_{bd}-\bar{A}_{ca}+\bar{A}_{cb}+\bar{A}_{da}-\bar{A}_{db})z_{a}z_{b}z_{c}^{3}z_{d}^{3}+(\bar{A}_{ba}-\bar{A}_{ca})z_{b}^{3}z_{c}^{3}z_{d}^{2}

\displaystyle+(\bar{A}_{da}-\bar{A}_{ba})z_{b}^{3}z_{c}^{2}z_{d}^{3}+(\bar{A}_{ca}-\bar{A}_{da})z_{b}^{2}z_{c}^{3}z_{d}^{3})/(z_{a}z_{b}z_{c}z_{d})\,,

where
1/z_{a}z_{b}z_{c}z_{d}
is not zero. The monomials
z_{a}^{u_{1}}z_{b}^{u_{2}}z_{c}^{u_{3}}z_{d}^{u_{4}}
(with
u_{1},u_{2},u_{3},u_{4}\in\{0,1,2,3,4\}
such that
u_{1}+u_{2}+u_{3}+u_{4}=8
) are independent and therefore, all the coefficients must be zero, yielding a linear system of 42 complex equations:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} A_{ca}-A_{da}&=0\phantom{\bar{A}}\\
A_{da}-A_{ba}&=0\phantom{\bar{A}}\\
A_{ba}-A_{ca}&=0\phantom{\bar{A}}\\
A_{db}-A_{cb}&=0\phantom{\bar{A}}\\
A_{ab}-A_{db}&=0\phantom{\bar{A}}\\
A_{cb}-A_{ab}&=0\phantom{\bar{A}}\\
A_{bc}-A_{dc}&=0\phantom{\bar{A}}\\
A_{dc}-A_{ac}&=0\phantom{\bar{A}}\\
A_{ac}-A_{bc}&=0\phantom{\bar{A}}\\
A_{cd}-A_{bd}&=0\phantom{\bar{A}}\\
A_{ad}-A_{cd}&=0\phantom{\bar{A}}\\
A_{bd}-A_{ad}&=0\phantom{\bar{A}}\end{aligned}\quad\quad\begin{aligned} \bar{A}_{ba}-\bar{A}_{ca}&=0\\
\bar{A}_{da}-\bar{A}_{ba}&=0\\
\bar{A}_{ca}-\bar{A}_{da}&=0\\
\bar{A}_{ab}-\bar{A}_{db}&=0\\
\bar{A}_{cb}-\bar{A}_{ab}&=0\\
\bar{A}_{db}-\bar{A}_{cb}&=0\\
\bar{A}_{dc}-\bar{A}_{ac}&=0\\
\bar{A}_{bc}-\bar{A}_{dc}&=0\\
\bar{A}_{ac}-\bar{A}_{bc}&=0\\
\bar{A}_{ad}-\bar{A}_{cd}&=0\\
\bar{A}_{bd}-\bar{A}_{ad}&=0\\
\bar{A}_{cd}-\bar{A}_{bd}&=0\end{aligned}\quad\quad\begin{aligned} A_{cb}-A_{ca}+A_{da}-A_{db}-\bar{A}_{ac}+\bar{A}_{ad}+\bar{A}_{bc}-\bar{A}_{bd}&=0\\
A_{cc}-A_{bb}+A_{db}-A_{dc}-\bar{A}_{ab}+\bar{A}_{ac}+\bar{A}_{bb}-\bar{A}_{cc}&=0\\
A_{bb}-A_{cb}+A_{cd}-A_{dd}+\bar{A}_{ab}-\bar{A}_{bb}-\bar{A}_{ad}+\bar{A}_{dd}&=0\\
A_{ba}-A_{bc}-A_{da}+A_{dc}+\bar{A}_{ab}-\bar{A}_{ad}-\bar{A}_{cb}+\bar{A}_{cd}&=0\\
A_{bc}-A_{bd}-A_{cc}+A_{dd}-\bar{A}_{ac}+\bar{A}_{ad}+\bar{A}_{cc}-\bar{A}_{dd}&=0\\
A_{bd}-A_{ba}+A_{ca}-A_{cd}-\bar{A}_{ab}+\bar{A}_{ac}+\bar{A}_{db}-\bar{A}_{dc}&=0\\
A_{aa}-A_{cc}-A_{da}+A_{dc}-\bar{A}_{aa}+\bar{A}_{ba}-\bar{A}_{bc}+\bar{A}_{cc}&=0\\
A_{ca}-A_{aa}-A_{cd}+A_{dd}+\bar{A}_{aa}-\bar{A}_{ba}+\bar{A}_{bd}-\bar{A}_{dd}&=0\\
A_{bb}-A_{aa}+A_{da}-A_{db}+\bar{A}_{aa}-\bar{A}_{bb}-\bar{A}_{ca}+\bar{A}_{cb}&=0\\
A_{aa}-A_{bb}-A_{ca}+A_{cb}-\bar{A}_{aa}+\bar{A}_{bb}+\bar{A}_{da}-\bar{A}_{db}&=0\\
A_{aa}-A_{ba}+A_{bd}-A_{dd}-\bar{A}_{aa}+\bar{A}_{ca}-\bar{A}_{cd}+\bar{A}_{dd}&=0\\
A_{ba}-A_{aa}-A_{bc}+A_{cc}+\bar{A}_{aa}-\bar{A}_{da}-\bar{A}_{cc}+\bar{A}_{dc}&=0\\
A_{ac}-A_{ab}+A_{db}-A_{dc}-\bar{A}_{ba}+\bar{A}_{ca}+\bar{A}_{bd}-\bar{A}_{cd}&=0\\
A_{ad}-A_{ac}+A_{cc}-A_{dd}+\bar{A}_{bc}-\bar{A}_{bd}-\bar{A}_{cc}+\bar{A}_{dd}&=0\\
A_{ab}-A_{ad}-A_{cb}+A_{cd}+\bar{A}_{ba}-\bar{A}_{bc}-\bar{A}_{da}+\bar{A}_{dc}&=0\\
A_{ab}-A_{ad}-A_{bb}+A_{dd}+\bar{A}_{bb}-\bar{A}_{cb}+\bar{A}_{cd}-\bar{A}_{dd}&=0\\
A_{ac}-A_{ab}+A_{bb}-A_{cc}-\bar{A}_{bb}+\bar{A}_{cc}+\bar{A}_{db}-\bar{A}_{dc}&=0\\
A_{ad}-A_{ac}+A_{bc}-A_{bd}-\bar{A}_{ca}+\bar{A}_{cb}+\bar{A}_{da}-\bar{A}_{db}&=0\end{aligned}

Half of the equations with two terms are complex conjugate of the other half and are thus redundant. This readily leads to the necessity of the first condition:

\displaystyle A_{ba}=A_{ca}

\displaystyle=A_{da}=:\mathcal{A}_{a}

(S76)

\displaystyle A_{ab}=A_{cb}

\displaystyle=A_{db}=:\mathcal{A}_{b}

\displaystyle A_{ac}=A_{bc}

\displaystyle=A_{dc}=:\mathcal{A}_{c}

\displaystyle A_{ad}=A_{bd}

\displaystyle=A_{cd}=:\mathcal{A}_{d}.

The equations with more than two terms can be rearranged as

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} A_{cb}-A_{db}+A_{da}-A_{ca}+\bar{A}_{bc}-\bar{A}_{ac}+\bar{A}_{ad}-\bar{A}_{bd}&=0\\
A_{cc}-A_{bb}+A_{db}-A_{dc}+\bar{A}_{bb}-\bar{A}_{ab}+\bar{A}_{ac}-\bar{A}_{cc}&=0\\
A_{bb}-A_{cb}+A_{cd}-A_{dd}+\bar{A}_{ab}-\bar{A}_{bb}+\bar{A}_{dd}-\bar{A}_{ad}&=0\\
A_{ba}-A_{da}+A_{dc}-A_{bc}+\bar{A}_{ab}-\bar{A}_{cb}+\bar{A}_{cd}-\bar{A}_{ad}&=0\\
A_{bc}-A_{cc}+A_{dd}-A_{bd}+\bar{A}_{cc}-\bar{A}_{ac}+\bar{A}_{ad}-\bar{A}_{dd}&=0\\
A_{bd}-A_{cd}+A_{ca}-A_{ba}+\bar{A}_{ac}-\bar{A}_{dc}+\bar{A}_{db}-\bar{A}_{ab}&=0\\
A_{aa}-A_{da}+A_{dc}-A_{cc}+\bar{A}_{ba}-\bar{A}_{aa}+\bar{A}_{cc}-\bar{A}_{bc}&=0\\
A_{ca}-A_{aa}+A_{dd}-A_{cd}+\bar{A}_{aa}-\bar{A}_{ba}+\bar{A}_{bd}-\bar{A}_{dd}&=0\\
A_{bb}-A_{db}+A_{da}-A_{aa}+\bar{A}_{aa}-\bar{A}_{ca}+\bar{A}_{cb}-\bar{A}_{bb}&=0\\
\end{aligned}\hskip 17.00024pt\begin{aligned} A_{aa}-A_{ca}+A_{cb}-A_{bb}+\bar{A}_{bb}-\bar{A}_{db}+\bar{A}_{da}-\bar{A}_{aa}&=0\\
A_{aa}-A_{ba}+A_{bd}-A_{dd}+\bar{A}_{ca}-\bar{A}_{aa}+\bar{A}_{dd}-\bar{A}_{cd}&=0\\
A_{ba}-A_{aa}+A_{cc}-A_{bc}+\bar{A}_{aa}-\bar{A}_{da}+\bar{A}_{dc}-\bar{A}_{cc}&=0\\
A_{ac}-A_{dc}+A_{db}-A_{ab}+\bar{A}_{ca}-\bar{A}_{ba}+\bar{A}_{bd}-\bar{A}_{cd}&=0\\
A_{ad}-A_{dd}+A_{cc}-A_{ac}+\bar{A}_{bc}-\bar{A}_{cc}+\bar{A}_{dd}-\bar{A}_{bd}&=0\\
A_{ab}-A_{cb}+A_{cd}-A_{ad}+\bar{A}_{ba}-\bar{A}_{da}+\bar{A}_{dc}-\bar{A}_{bc}&=0\\
A_{ab}-A_{bb}+A_{dd}-A_{ad}+\bar{A}_{bb}-\bar{A}_{cb}+\bar{A}_{cd}-\bar{A}_{dd}&=0\\
A_{ac}-A_{cc}+A_{bb}-A_{ab}+\bar{A}_{cc}-\bar{A}_{dc}+\bar{A}_{db}-\bar{A}_{bb}&=0\\
A_{ad}-A_{bd}+A_{bc}-A_{ac}+\bar{A}_{da}-\bar{A}_{ca}+\bar{A}_{cb}-\bar{A}_{db}&=0\,.\end{aligned}

Using Eqs. (
S76
) cancels every equation from the latter system that does not involve a self-interaction term
A_{jj}
for some
j\in\{a,b,c,d\}
. This leads to

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} A_{cc}-A_{dc}+A_{db}-A_{bb}+\bar{A}_{bb}-\bar{A}_{ab}+\bar{A}_{ac}-\bar{A}_{cc}&=0\\
A_{bb}-A_{cb}+A_{cd}-A_{dd}+\bar{A}_{ab}-\bar{A}_{bb}+\bar{A}_{dd}-\bar{A}_{ad}&=0\\
A_{bc}-A_{cc}+A_{dd}-A_{bd}+\bar{A}_{cc}-\bar{A}_{ac}+\bar{A}_{ad}-\bar{A}_{dd}&=0\\
A_{aa}-A_{da}+A_{dc}-A_{cc}+\bar{A}_{ba}-\bar{A}_{aa}+\bar{A}_{cc}-\bar{A}_{bc}&=0\\
A_{ca}-A_{aa}+A_{dd}-A_{cd}+\bar{A}_{aa}-\bar{A}_{ba}+\bar{A}_{bd}-\bar{A}_{dd}&=0\\
A_{bb}-A_{db}+A_{da}-A_{aa}+\bar{A}_{aa}-\bar{A}_{ca}+\bar{A}_{cb}-\bar{A}_{bb}&=0\end{aligned}\hskip 17.00024pt\begin{aligned} A_{aa}-A_{ca}+A_{cb}-A_{bb}+\bar{A}_{bb}-\bar{A}_{db}+\bar{A}_{da}-\bar{A}_{aa}&=0\\
A_{aa}-A_{ba}+A_{bd}-A_{dd}+\bar{A}_{ca}-\bar{A}_{aa}+\bar{A}_{dd}-\bar{A}_{cd}&=0\\
A_{ba}-A_{aa}+A_{cc}-A_{bc}+\bar{A}_{aa}-\bar{A}_{da}+\bar{A}_{dc}-\bar{A}_{cc}&=0\\
A_{ad}-A_{dd}+A_{cc}-A_{ac}+\bar{A}_{bc}-\bar{A}_{cc}+\bar{A}_{dd}-\bar{A}_{bd}&=0\\
A_{ab}-A_{bb}+A_{dd}-A_{ad}+\bar{A}_{bb}-\bar{A}_{cb}+\bar{A}_{cd}-\bar{A}_{dd}&=0\\
A_{ac}-A_{cc}+A_{bb}-A_{ab}+\bar{A}_{cc}-\bar{A}_{dc}+\bar{A}_{db}-\bar{A}_{bb}&=0\,.\end{aligned}

Half of these equations are the complex conjugate of the other half and are thus redundant:

\displaystyle A_{cc}-A_{dc}+A_{db}-A_{bb}+\bar{A}_{bb}-\bar{A}_{ab}+\bar{A}_{ac}-\bar{A}_{cc}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle A_{bb}-A_{cb}+A_{cd}-A_{dd}+\bar{A}_{ab}-\bar{A}_{bb}+\bar{A}_{dd}-\bar{A}_{ad}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle A_{bc}-A_{cc}+A_{dd}-A_{bd}+\bar{A}_{cc}-\bar{A}_{ac}+\bar{A}_{ad}-\bar{A}_{dd}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle A_{aa}-A_{da}+A_{dc}-A_{cc}+\bar{A}_{ba}-\bar{A}_{aa}+\bar{A}_{cc}-\bar{A}_{bc}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle A_{ca}-A_{aa}+A_{dd}-A_{cd}+\bar{A}_{aa}-\bar{A}_{ba}+\bar{A}_{bd}-\bar{A}_{dd}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle A_{bb}-A_{db}+A_{da}-A_{aa}+\bar{A}_{aa}-\bar{A}_{ca}+\bar{A}_{cb}-\bar{A}_{bb}

\displaystyle=0\,.

Using Eqs. (
S76
) once again to express the latter equations in terms of
\mathcal{A}_{a},\mathcal{A}_{b},\mathcal{A}_{c},\mathcal{A}_{d}
makes it evident that they are constraints for the self-interaction parameters:

\displaystyle(A_{cc}-\bar{A}_{cc})-(A_{bb}-\bar{A}_{bb})

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{A}_{c}-\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{c})-(\mathcal{A}_{b}-\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{b})

\displaystyle(A_{bb}-\bar{A}_{bb})-(A_{dd}-\bar{A}_{dd})

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{A}_{b}-\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{b})-(\mathcal{A}_{d}-\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{d})

\displaystyle(A_{dd}-\bar{A}_{dd})-(A_{cc}-\bar{A}_{cc})

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{A}_{d}-\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{d})-(\mathcal{A}_{c}-\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{c})

\displaystyle(A_{aa}-\bar{A}_{aa})-(A_{cc}-\bar{A}_{cc})

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{A}_{a}-\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{a})-(\mathcal{A}_{c}-\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{c})

\displaystyle(A_{dd}-\bar{A}_{dd})-(A_{aa}-\bar{A}_{aa})

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{A}_{d}-\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{d})-(\mathcal{A}_{a}-\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{a})

\displaystyle(A_{bb}-\bar{A}_{bb})-(A_{aa}-\bar{A}_{aa})

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{A}_{b}-\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{b})-(\mathcal{A}_{a}-\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{a})\,.

The equations can be written in real form as

\displaystyle\Imag(A_{cc}-A_{bb})

\displaystyle=\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{c}-\mathcal{A}_{b})

\displaystyle\Imag(A_{bb}-A_{dd})

\displaystyle=\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{b}-\mathcal{A}_{d})

\displaystyle\Imag(A_{dd}-A_{cc})

\displaystyle=\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{d}-\mathcal{A}_{c})

\displaystyle\Imag(A_{aa}-A_{cc})

\displaystyle=\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{a}-\mathcal{A}_{c})

\displaystyle\Imag(A_{dd}-A_{aa})

\displaystyle=\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{d}-\mathcal{A}_{a})

\displaystyle\Imag(A_{bb}-A_{aa})

\displaystyle=\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{b}-\mathcal{A}_{a})\,.

One observes that only three of them are independent, which leads to

\displaystyle\Imag(A_{bb}-A_{aa})=\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{b}-\mathcal{A}_{a})\,,\quad\Imag(A_{cc}-A_{aa})=\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{c}-\mathcal{A}_{a})\,,\quad\Imag(A_{dd}-A_{aa})=\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{d}-\mathcal{A}_{a})\,,

and

\displaystyle\omega_{b}-\omega_{a}=2\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{b}-\mathcal{A}_{a})\,,\qquad\omega_{c}-\omega_{a}=2\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{c}-\mathcal{A}_{a})\,,\qquad\omega_{d}-\omega_{a}=2\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{d}-\mathcal{A}_{a})\,.

Combining these finally provides the third condition

\displaystyle\omega_{a}-2\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{a})=\omega_{b}-2\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{b})=\omega_{c}-2\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{c})=\omega_{d}-2\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{d})\,.

Altogether, the three conditions are necessary and sufficient to have
\mathcal{K}[c_{abcd}](\boldsymbol{z})=0
and the proof of the theorem is complete.

Remark S16.
It may be surprising to observe that the natural frequencies do not have to be identical when nontrivial phase lags are present. Yet, as shown in subsection
S4.5
, the third condition in fact guarantees that the oscillators whose positions on the unit circle participate in a conserved cross-ratio have the same effective frequency. Also, in terms of the original parameters, the third condition is equivalent to

\displaystyle\omega_{a}-W_{k_{a}a}\sin\alpha_{k_{a}a}=\omega_{b}-W_{k_{b}b}\sin\alpha_{k_{b}b}=\omega_{c}-W_{k_{c}c}\sin\alpha_{k_{c}c}=\omega_{d}-W_{k_{d}d}\sin\alpha_{k_{d}d}\,,

where
k_{j}
takes any value within
\{a,b,c,d\}\setminus\{j\}
for
j\in\{a,b,c,d\}
.

Remark S17.
Note that there is no restriction on the outgoing edges from the vertices involved in conserved cross-ratios to the vertices not involved in a conserved cross-ratio. Indeed, although the conditions of the theorem make the equations for the oscillators admitting a conserved cross-ratio identical, their initial conditions typically differ and their contributions within the whole network are generally heterogeneous.

Remark S18.
If the phases are not equal at time
t=0
and are involved in a conserved cross-ratio, the cross-ratio is not zero. Since it is conserved, the cross-ratio remains non-zero throughout time and the phases cannot cross (become equal) for all finite values of
t

[
81
]
.

S4.5
Basic examples for Theorem 3

One of the simplest, but instructive, examples with a conserved cross-ratio is the following.

Example S19.
Consider a graph of
N=5
vertices with complex weight matrix

\displaystyle A=\begin{pmatrix}i\omega_{1}/2&\mathcal{A}_{2}&\mathcal{A}_{3}&\mathcal{A}_{4}&\mathcal{A}_{5}\\
\mathcal{A}_{1}&i\omega_{2}/2&\mathcal{A}_{3}&\mathcal{A}_{4}&\mathcal{A}_{5}\\
\mathcal{A}_{1}&\mathcal{A}_{2}&i\omega_{3}/2&\mathcal{A}_{4}&\mathcal{A}_{5}\\
\mathcal{A}_{1}&\mathcal{A}_{2}&\mathcal{A}_{3}&i\omega_{4}/2&\mathcal{A}_{5}\\
A_{51}&A_{52}&A_{53}&A_{54}&i\omega/2\end{pmatrix}\,,

where
\omega
and
\omega_{1}
are fixed to arbitrary real values while

\displaystyle\omega_{j}=\omega_{1}+2\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{j}-\mathcal{A}_{1})\,,\quad j\in\{2,3,4\}\,.

By construction, the effective natural frequency of oscillators 2, 3, 4 is
\Omega=\omega_{1}-2\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{1})
. In fact, one can readily verify that this yields identical equations for oscillators 1,2,3,4 (although they have different contributions to oscillator 5):
\dot{z}_{j}=\rho(\boldsymbol{z})+i\Omega z_{j}-\overline{\rho(\boldsymbol{z})}z_{j}^{2}
where
j\in\{1,2,3,4\}
and
\rho(\boldsymbol{z})=\sum_{k=1}^{N}\mathcal{A}_{k}z_{k}
. In such a case, from Thm.
S4.4
and Lem.
S4.3
, there is only one functionally independent cross-ratio, say

\displaystyle c_{1234}(z)=\frac{(z_{3}-z_{1})(z_{4}-z_{2})}{(z_{3}-z_{2})(z_{4}-z_{1})}\,,

that is conserved.

Let’s now present a more general example in terms of network structure.

Example S20.
Consider a graph with
N=13
vertices with complex weight matrix

\displaystyle A=\begin{pmatrix}i\omega_{1}/2&\mathcal{A}_{1,2}&\mathcal{A}_{1,3}&\mathcal{A}_{1,4}&\mathcal{A}_{1,5}&\mathcal{A}_{1,6}&\mathcal{A}_{1,7}&\mathcal{A}_{1,8}&\mathcal{A}_{1,9}&\mathcal{A}_{1,10}&\mathcal{A}_{1,11}&\mathcal{A}_{1,12}&\mathcal{A}_{1,13}\\
\mathcal{A}_{1,1}&i\omega_{2}/2&\mathcal{A}_{1,3}&\mathcal{A}_{1,4}&\mathcal{A}_{1,5}&\mathcal{A}_{1,6}&\mathcal{A}_{1,7}&\mathcal{A}_{1,8}&\mathcal{A}_{1,9}&\mathcal{A}_{1,10}&\mathcal{A}_{1,11}&\mathcal{A}_{1,12}&\mathcal{A}_{1,13}\\
\mathcal{A}_{1,1}&\mathcal{A}_{1,2}&i\omega_{3}/2&\mathcal{A}_{1,4}&\mathcal{A}_{1,5}&\mathcal{A}_{1,6}&\mathcal{A}_{1,7}&\mathcal{A}_{1,8}&\mathcal{A}_{1,9}&\mathcal{A}_{1,10}&\mathcal{A}_{1,11}&\mathcal{A}_{1,12}&\mathcal{A}_{1,13}\\
\mathcal{A}_{1,1}&\mathcal{A}_{1,2}&\mathcal{A}_{1,3}&i\omega_{4}/2&\mathcal{A}_{1,5}&\mathcal{A}_{1,6}&\mathcal{A}_{1,7}&\mathcal{A}_{1,8}&\mathcal{A}_{1,9}&\mathcal{A}_{1,10}&\mathcal{A}_{1,11}&\mathcal{A}_{1,12}&\mathcal{A}_{1,13}\\
\hline\cr\mathcal{A}_{2,1}&\mathcal{A}_{2,2}&\mathcal{A}_{2,3}&\mathcal{A}_{2,4}&i\omega_{5}/2&\mathcal{A}_{2,6}&\mathcal{A}_{2,7}&\mathcal{A}_{2,8}&\mathcal{A}_{2,9}&\mathcal{A}_{2,10}&\mathcal{A}_{2,11}&\mathcal{A}_{2,12}&\mathcal{A}_{2,13}\\
\mathcal{A}_{2,1}&\mathcal{A}_{2,2}&\mathcal{A}_{2,3}&\mathcal{A}_{2,4}&\mathcal{A}_{2,5}&i\omega_{6}/2&\mathcal{A}_{2,7}&\mathcal{A}_{2,8}&\mathcal{A}_{2,9}&\mathcal{A}_{2,10}&\mathcal{A}_{2,11}&\mathcal{A}_{2,12}&\mathcal{A}_{2,13}\\
\mathcal{A}_{2,1}&\mathcal{A}_{2,2}&\mathcal{A}_{2,3}&\mathcal{A}_{2,4}&\mathcal{A}_{2,5}&\mathcal{A}_{2,6}&i\omega_{7}/2&\mathcal{A}_{2,8}&\mathcal{A}_{2,9}&\mathcal{A}_{2,10}&\mathcal{A}_{2,11}&\mathcal{A}_{2,12}&\mathcal{A}_{2,13}\\
\mathcal{A}_{2,1}&\mathcal{A}_{2,2}&\mathcal{A}_{2,3}&\mathcal{A}_{2,4}&\mathcal{A}_{2,5}&\mathcal{A}_{2,6}&\mathcal{A}_{2,7}&i\omega_{8}/2&\mathcal{A}_{2,9}&\mathcal{A}_{2,10}&\mathcal{A}_{2,11}&\mathcal{A}_{2,12}&\mathcal{A}_{2,13}\\
\mathcal{A}_{2,1}&\mathcal{A}_{2,2}&\mathcal{A}_{2,3}&\mathcal{A}_{2,4}&\mathcal{A}_{2,5}&\mathcal{A}_{2,6}&\mathcal{A}_{2,7}&\mathcal{A}_{2,8}&i\omega_{9}/2&\mathcal{A}_{2,10}&\mathcal{A}_{2,11}&\mathcal{A}_{2,12}&\mathcal{A}_{2,13}\\
\mathcal{A}_{2,1}&\mathcal{A}_{2,2}&\mathcal{A}_{2,3}&\mathcal{A}_{2,4}&\mathcal{A}_{2,5}&\mathcal{A}_{2,6}&\mathcal{A}_{2,7}&\mathcal{A}_{2,8}&\mathcal{A}_{2,9}&i\omega_{10}/2&\mathcal{A}_{2,11}&\mathcal{A}_{2,12}&\mathcal{A}_{2,13}\\
\hline\cr A_{11,1}&A_{11,2}&A_{11,3}&A_{11,4}&A_{11,5}&A_{11,6}&A_{11,7}&A_{11,8}&A_{11,9}&A_{11,10}&i\omega_{11}/2&A_{11,12}&A_{11,13}\\
A_{12,1}&A_{12,2}&A_{12,3}&A_{12,4}&A_{12,5}&A_{12,6}&A_{12,7}&A_{12,8}&A_{12,9}&A_{12,10}&A_{12,11}&i\omega_{12}/2&A_{12,13}\\
A_{13,1}&A_{13,2}&A_{13,3}&A_{13,4}&A_{13,5}&A_{13,6}&A_{13,7}&A_{13,8}&A_{13,9}&A_{13,10}&A_{13,11}&A_{13,12}&i\omega_{13}/2\end{pmatrix}\,,

where

\displaystyle\omega_{j}=\begin{cases}\text{arbitrary real number}&\text{if }j\in\{1,5,11,12,13\},\\
\omega_{1}+2\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{1,j}-\mathcal{A}_{1,1})&\text{if }j\in\{2,3,4\},\\
\omega_{5}+2\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{2,j}-\mathcal{A}_{2,5})&\text{if }j\in\{6,7,8,9,10\}.\end{cases}

The effective natural frequencies within each partially integrable part are
\Omega_{1}=\omega_{1}-2\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{1,1})
and
\Omega_{2}=\omega_{5}-2\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{2,5})
. Following Thm.
S4.4
and Lem.
S4.3
, the following cross-ratios are functionally independent constants of motion:

\displaystyle c_{1,2,3,4}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{(z_{3}-z_{1})(z_{4}-z_{2})}{(z_{3}-z_{2})(z_{4}-z_{1})}

related to the first four oscillators and

\displaystyle c_{5,6,7,8}(z)=\frac{(z_{7}-z_{5})(z_{8}-z_{6})}{(z_{7}-z_{6})(z_{8}-z_{5})}\,,\quad c_{6,7,8,9}(z)=\frac{(z_{8}-z_{6})(z_{9}-z_{7})}{(z_{8}-z_{7})(z_{9}-z_{6})}\,,\quad c_{7,8,9,10}(z)=\frac{(z_{9}-z_{7})(z_{10}-z_{8})}{(z_{9}-z_{8})(z_{10}-z_{7})}

for oscillators 5 to 10.

Figure S2
:
Simplified history of weight matrices in WS theory
. Previous results on the possible forms of weight matrices allowing conserved cross-ratios and a generic example of weight matrix satisfying the conditions of Thm.
S4.4
, without considering natural frequencies and phase lags for simplicity.

In Fig.
S2
, we illustrate the weight matrix of a more general network of Kuramoto oscillators with conserved cross-ratios.

S4.6
Corollaries of Theorem 3

In this subsection, we provide some consequences of Thm.
S4.4
. First, the theorem readily gives the necessary and sufficient conditions to have
N-3
constants of motion having the form of cross-ratios. The sufficiency is known from the excellent work of Lohe
[
81
,
83
]
. The following corollary formalizes this result while adding the necessity of the conditions.

Corollary S21.

The
N
-dimensional Kuramoto model on a graph with complex weight matrix
A
admits the maximum number of functionally independent cross-ratios as constants of motion, namely
N-3
, if and only if the following two conditions are satisfied:

1.

A_{j\ell}=A_{k\ell}=:\mathcal{A}_{\ell}
for all
\ell\in\{1,...,N\}
and for all pairs
(j,k)
with
j,k\in\{1,...,N\}
and
k,\ell\neq j\,;

2.

\omega_{j}-2\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{j})=\omega_{k}-2\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{k})
for all pairs
(j,k)
with
j,k\in\{1,...,N\}
and
k\neq j\,.

Proof.
(
\Leftarrow
) Assume that the first condition of the corollary holds. Then, conditions 1 and 2 of Thm.
S4.4
are automatically satisfied. Moreover, if the second condition of the corollary also holds, then condition 3 of Thm.
S4.4
is satisfied as well.
Therefore, a Kuramoto system that satisfies the two conditions of the corollary admits each cross-ratio
c_{abcd}
as a constant of motion.
Now, according to Lem.
S4.3
, only
N-3
of them can be functionally independent, meaning that
N-3
is the maximal number of functionally independent cross-ratios being constants of motion.

(
\Rightarrow
) We will prove the contrapositive. Let
(j,k)
, where
j,k\in\{1,\cdots,N\}
and
k\neq j
, be a pair for which condition 1 or condition 2 of the corollary is not satisfied.
Without loss of generality, relabel the oscillators in such a way that
j=N-1
and
k=N
.
Consider the cross-ratio
c_{(N-3)(N-2)(N-1)N}
.
This cross-ratio cannot be conserved, because condition 1 of the corollary not being satisfied implies that condition 1 of Thm.
S4.4
is not satisfied, and 2 of the corollary not being satisfied implies that condition 3 of Thm.
S4.4
is not satisfied.
Moreover, by the same reasoning, any cross-ratio involving
N-1
and
N
cannot be conserved.
Consider the
N-4
cross-ratios in

\{c_{1234},c_{2345},...,c_{(N-4)(N-3)(N-2)(N-1)}\}.

(S77)

According to Lem.
S4.3
, any
c_{abcd}
with
a,b,c,d\in\{1,\cdots,N-1\}
is functionally dependent on those
N-4
cross-ratios, so any additional independent cross-ratio must involve oscillator
N
.
Since all permutations of the indices of a cross-ratio are functionally dependent, consider without loss of generality that this new independent cross-ratio is
c_{aNbc}
, where
a,b,c\in\{1,\cdots,N-1\}
.
However, the
N-3
cross-ratios in

\{c_{1234},c_{2345},...,c_{(N-4)(N-3)(N-2)(N-1)},c_{aNbc}\}

(S78)

cannot all be conserved. Indeed, consider the cross-ratio
c_{(N-1)abc}
, which is dependent on the
N-4
first cross-ratios. Then, by Eq. (
S60
),

c_{(N-1)abc}\,c_{aNbc}=c_{(N-1)Nbc}\,,

(S79)

but recall that any cross-ratio involving oscillators
N-1
and
N
cannot be conserved. Therefore, if either condition 1 or condition 2 is not satisfied, then the model cannot admit
N-3
conserved cross-ratios.

From a graph-theoretical perspective, the latter corollary implies that graphs other than the complete graph or the star graph also admit
N-3
conserved cross-ratios. Consider the following simple example.

Example S22.
Consider binary matrices
A
satisfying the first condition of Corollary
S4.6
, disregarding the diagonal. There are
2^{N}
such matrices, corresponding to all possible binary choices for each of the
N
columns. Each of these matrices defines a graph. Between them, there are many graph isomorphisms. Starting from the complete graph, for which all matrix elements are equal to 1: if one changes no column, there is 1 possible graph; if one changes a column of ones into a column of zeros, there are
N
isomorphic graphs; if one changes two such columns, there are
\binom{N}{2}
isomorphic graphs, etc. Generally, if one changes
k
columns of ones into columns of zeros, there are
\binom{N}{k}
isomorphic graphs. Summing over all the isomorphic graphs yields the total number of possibilities from the binomial theorem
\sum_{k=0}^{N}\binom{N}{k}=2^{N}
. There are thus
N
non-isomorphic, weakly connected, binary graphs leading to
N-3
conserved cross-ratios in the Kuramoto dynamics. The 16 four-vertex graphs and the 32 five-vertex graphs including isomorphisms, all supporting the maximal number of conserved cross-ratios, are presented in Fig.
S3
and Fig.
S4
.

Figure S3
:
4-vertices motifs admitting a conserved cross-ratio
(considering that the conditions on the frequencies and the phase lags are satisfied). The number of arcs (oriented edges)
M
is specified above the graph while the number of isomorphic graphs is specified below the graphs. Note that there are 0, 1, 2, 3, 0 vertices in a “source subgraph” respectively for
M=0,3,6,9,12
, allowing the possibility of having monomial eigenfunctions if all the conditions of Thm.
S2.1
are satisfied.

Figure S4
:
5-vertices motifs admitting two conserved cross-ratios
. All 32 possible binary graphs with 5 vertices admitting the maximal number of 2 functionally independent conserved cross-ratios in the corresponding Kuramoto model (considering that the conditions on the frequencies and the phase lags are satisfied). The 6 non-isomorphic graphs are displayed vertically and the corresponding isomorphisms are displayed horizontally.

The next corollary is the equivalent of the theorem when there is no phase lag between the oscillators.

Corollary S23.

Consider the
N
-dimensional Kuramoto model (
S1
) on a graph described by some
N\times N
real matrix
W
, with natural frequencies
(\omega_{j})_{j=1}^{N}
and zero phase lags. The cross-ratio
c_{abcd}
(
S39
) is a constant of motion in the model if and only if the vertices
a
,
b
,
c
, and
d
have the same:

1.

outgoing edges within
\{a,b,c,d\}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle W_{ba}=W_{ca}=W_{da}\,,\quad W_{ab}=W_{cb}=W_{db}\,,\quad W_{ac}=W_{bc}=W_{dc}\,,\quad W_{ad}=W_{bd}=W_{cd}\,;

(S80)

2.

incoming edges from the vertices outside
\{a,b,c,d\}
in the graph, i.e.,

W_{ak}=W_{bk}=W_{ck}=W_{dk},\quad\forall\,k\in\{1,...,N\}\setminus\{a,b,c,d\}\,;

(S81)

3.

natural frequencies, i.e.,

\omega_{a}=\omega_{b}=\omega_{c}=\omega_{d}\,;

(S82)

Proof.
When the phase lags are zero,
A=\frac{1}{2}(W+i\,\mathrm{diag}(\boldsymbol{\omega}))
. In this case, the first two conditions of Thm.
S4.4
, which only involve non-diagonal terms of
A
, coincide with the first two conditions stated in the corollary. Using the explicit form of the third condition in Thm.
S4.4
, that is,

\displaystyle\omega_{a}-W_{k_{a}a}\sin\alpha_{k_{a}a}=\omega_{b}-W_{k_{b}b}\sin\alpha_{k_{b}b}=\omega_{c}-W_{k_{c}c}\sin\alpha_{k_{c}c}=\omega_{d}-W_{k_{d}d}\sin\alpha_{k_{d}d}\,,

where
k_{j}
takes any value within
\{a,b,c,d\}\setminus\{j\}
for
j\in\{a,b,c,d\}
, the sine terms vanish due to the zero phase lags. This directly yields
\omega_{a}=\omega_{b}=\omega_{c}=\omega_{d}
, as stated in the third condition of the corollary.

Remark S24.
The latter corollary was also verified with symbolic calculations in Matlab (
symbolic_calculations_theorem.m
) and in Mathematica (
KMK_constants_of_motion.nb
) in Ref.
[
144
]
.

In the following corollary, we only focus on the weight matrix conditions to admit a conserved cross-ratio when there is a star
S_{N}
with
N
vertices in an undirected graph.

Corollary S25.

Consider the Kuramoto model (
1
) on an undirected graph where the phase lags are zero and the graph contains a star
S_{n}
of
n
vertices with peripheral oscillators having identical frequencies and only a connection to the core. Then,
n
must be at least 5 for the star to admit a conserved cross-ratio.

Proof.
The cross-ratio involves 4 vertices and the stars
S_{1}
(trivial graph),
S_{2}
(path), or
S_{3}
(path) are readily excluded. For
n=4
, denote the core by
a
and the periphery by
\{b,c,d\}
without loss of generality. The core is connected to all vertices in the periphery, so in particular,
W_{ab}=1
. However,
W_{cb}=0\neq W_{ab}
and thus the first condition in Corollary
S4.6
is not satisfied.

For
n=5
, let the core be labeled
e
and the periphery
\{a,b,c,d\}
. The first condition is readily satisfied since there is no edge between the peripheral vertices in Corollary
S4.6
. The second condition in Corollary
S4.6
is also satisfied since
W_{ae}=W_{be}=W_{ce}=W_{de}=1
. Setting the natural frequencies of the vertices
a,b,c,d
to be identical, the Kuramoto model on the star
S_{5}
admits the cross-ratio
c_{abcd}
as a constant of motion by Corollary
S4.6
.

Remark S26.
In the directed case, the smallest star that admits a conserved cross-ratio is composed of 4 vertices, as shown in Fig.
S3
.

S4.7
WS integrals to Darboux functions

For
\omega_{j}=0
,
\alpha_{jk}=\delta-\pi/2
with
\sin\delta=0
, and
W_{jk}=1/N
for all
j,k\in\mathcal{V}
, the model admits
N-2
functionally independent constants of motion of the form

\displaystyle C^{\mathrm{ws}}_{j_{1}...j_{N}}=S_{j_{1}j_{2}}S_{j_{2}j_{3}}...S_{j_{N-1}j_{N}}S_{j_{N}j_{1}}\,,

(S83)

where
S_{jk}=\sin\left((\theta_{j}-\theta_{k})/2\right)
. J.W. Swift found these integrals for
N=3,\,4
and conjectured the general case, which was then proved by Watanabe and Strogatz
[
156
,
157
,
45
]
. They are now often called “WS integrals”. As noted in Ref.
[
157
]
, these constants of motion are Hénon-type integrals: they are indexed by cyclic labels and are built as products of sine factors, reminiscent of the product-type integrals in Toda lattices
[
55
,
38
,
147
]
.

Cross-ratios are directly linked to WS integrals
[
88
]
:

\displaystyle c_{abcd}=-\frac{C^{\mathrm{ws}}_{acdb\,j_{5}...j_{N}}}{C^{\mathrm{ws}}_{adcb\,j_{5}...j_{N}}}=\frac{S_{ca}S_{db}}{S_{cb}S_{da}}\,.

(S84)

Although Eq.
S84
was established in Ref.
[
88
]
in the context where
\omega_{j}=0
,
\alpha_{jk}=\delta-\pi/2
,
\sin\delta=0
, and
W_{jk}=1/N
for all
j,k
, the relation is always valid. Therefore, one might ask what happens when the conditions of Thm.
1.3
are satisfied or, more generally, for any dynamics whose Koopman generator has the form

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}=\alpha(t,\boldsymbol{z})L_{-1}+\beta(t,\boldsymbol{z})L_{0}+\gamma(t,\boldsymbol{z})L_{1}\,.

(S85)

Are the WS quantities in Eq. (
S83
) first integrals under these conditions ?

Theorem
1.3
and Eq. (
S85
) do not imply that the WS integrals are conserved even if the relation
c_{abcd}=-C^{\mathrm{ws}}_{acdb\,j_{5}...j_{\ell}}/C^{\mathrm{ws}}_{adcb\,j_{5}...j_{\ell}}
for
4\leq\ell\leq N
still holds. They do suggest that
C^{\mathrm{ws}}_{acdb\,j_{5}...j_{\ell}}
and
C^{\mathrm{ws}}_{adcb\,j_{5}...j_{\ell}}
may be eigenfunctions of the Koopman generator
\mathcal{K}
such that the ratio of these eigenfunctions yields another eigenfunction of null eigenvalue—i.e., a constant of motion—similarly to the cancellation of the eigenvalues using monomial and Vandermonde-ratio eigenfunctions.
In fact, WS quantities in Eq. (
S83
) are, however, more general Darboux functions
[
53
,
166
]
of the form

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}[C^{\mathrm{ws}}_{\boldsymbol{j}}(\boldsymbol{z})]=\Lambda_{\boldsymbol{j}}(\boldsymbol{z})\,C^{\mathrm{ws}}_{\boldsymbol{j}}(\boldsymbol{z})\,,

where
\Lambda_{\boldsymbol{j}}(\boldsymbol{z})
is called a cofactor,
C^{\mathrm{ws}}_{\boldsymbol{j}}(\boldsymbol{z})=\prod_{u=1}^{\ell}(1-z_{j_{u}}\bar{z}_{j_{u+1}})
is the complex form of the WS function, and
\boldsymbol{j}=(j_{1},...,j_{\ell})
with
j_{\ell+1}=j_{1}
. Unlike Koopman eigenfunctions, having two Darboux functions
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}
with (non constant) cofactors
\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2}
does not imply a conserved quantity, since
\mathcal{K}[\psi_{1}^{a_{1}}(\boldsymbol{z})\psi_{2}^{a_{2}}(\boldsymbol{z})]=(a_{1}\Lambda_{1}(\boldsymbol{z})+a_{2}\Lambda_{2}(\boldsymbol{z}))\psi_{1}^{a_{1}}(\boldsymbol{z})\psi_{2}^{a_{2}}(\boldsymbol{z})
and the real constants
a_{1},a_{2}
cannot necessarily be chosen to cancel the contributions of
\Lambda_{1}(\boldsymbol{z})
and
\Lambda_{2}(\boldsymbol{z})
.

The particularity of WS functions is that they share the same cofactor whenever the Koopman generator has the form in Eq. (
S85
). Indeed,

\displaystyle L_{-1}[\ln C^{\mathrm{ws}}_{\boldsymbol{j}}(\boldsymbol{z})]=-\textstyle{\sum_{u=1}^{\ell}}\bar{z}_{j_{u}}\,,\qquad\quad L_{0}[\ln C^{\mathrm{ws}}_{\boldsymbol{j}}(\boldsymbol{z})]=0\,,\qquad\quad L_{1}[\ln C^{\mathrm{ws}}_{\boldsymbol{j}}(\boldsymbol{z})]=\textstyle{\sum_{u=1}^{\ell}}z_{j_{u}}\,.

Thus, for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
,
\boldsymbol{z}\in\mathbb{T}^{N}
, and for a fixed set of labels
{j_{1},\ldots,j_{\ell}}
, the cofactor is

\displaystyle\Lambda(\boldsymbol{z}):=\Lambda_{\boldsymbol{j}}(\boldsymbol{z})=\gamma(t,\boldsymbol{z})\sum_{u=1}^{\ell}z_{j_{u}}-\alpha(t,\boldsymbol{z})\sum_{u=1}^{\ell}\bar{z}_{j_{u}}

(S86)

for any given ordering of the labels
{j_{1},\ldots,j_{\ell}}
, which is a consequence of the fact that
\Lambda_{\boldsymbol{j}}(\boldsymbol{z})
depends on symmetric polynomials and functions
\alpha,\gamma
do not depend on the indices
j_{1},...,j_{\ell}
. Therefore, it suffices to take the ratio of two WS (Darboux) functions to get the conserved cross-ratio.

In the specific case of the Kuramoto model, the conditions of Thm.
1.3
are satisfied and the cofactor becomes

\displaystyle\Lambda(\boldsymbol{z})=-2\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left[\left(\sum_{u=1}^{\ell}\bar{z}_{j_{u}}\right)\left(\sum_{k=1}^{N}\mathcal{A}_{k}z_{k}\right)\right]\,,

(S87)

where
\mathcal{A}_{1},...,\mathcal{A}_{N}\in\mathbb{C}
. If
\mathcal{A}_{k}=\frac{w_{k}}{2}e^{-i\alpha_{k}}
with
w_{k},\alpha_{k}\in\mathbb{R}
, the real form of the cofactor is

\displaystyle\tilde{\Lambda}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=-\sum_{u=1}^{\ell}\sum_{k=1}^{N}w_{k}\cos(\theta_{k}-\theta_{j_{u}}-\alpha_{k})\,.

From the cofactor in Eq. (
S86
), the general condition to have a WS eigenfunction or integral (
\lambda=0
[Lem.
S5.8
]) is therefore

\displaystyle\gamma(t,\boldsymbol{z})\sum_{u=1}^{\ell}z_{j_{u}}-\alpha(t,\boldsymbol{z})\sum_{u=1}^{\ell}\bar{z}_{j_{u}}=\lambda\in\mathbb{C}\,.

(S88)

Since
L_{0}[\ln C^{\mathrm{ws}}_{\boldsymbol{j}}(\boldsymbol{z})]=0
(i.e., the WS function is an invariant of
L_{0}
), there is no restriction on
\beta(t,\boldsymbol{z})
to conserve the WS function. Hence, any dynamics
\dot{z}_{j}=\alpha(t,\boldsymbol{z})+\beta(t,\boldsymbol{z})z_{j}+\gamma(t,\boldsymbol{z})z_{j}^{2}
satisfying Eq. (
S88
) with
\lambda=0
will admit WS integrals. More explicitly, with
\alpha^{\prime}(t,\boldsymbol{z}):=\alpha(t,\boldsymbol{z})/(\sum_{u=1}^{\ell}z_{j_{u}})
and
L^{\prime}:=(\sum_{u=1}^{\ell}z_{j_{u}})L_{-1}+(\sum_{u=1}^{\ell}\bar{z}_{j_{u}})L_{1}
, one gets
\mathcal{K}_{\alpha^{\prime},\beta}=\alpha^{\prime}(t,\boldsymbol{z})L^{\prime}+\beta(t,\boldsymbol{z})L_{0}
, the WS quantity is a joint invariant of
L^{\prime}
and
L_{0}
, and it is thus conserved.

S5
Continuous symmetries and the generation of new constants of motion

The concept of symmetry for differential equations has a long history that has flourished from the work of Sophus Lie to the work of Emmy Noether. Below, we only briefly present the theory for ordinary differential equations in order to present the symmetry criterion under Koopman’s perspective and dive quickly into its application to the Kuramoto model. For more details, the reader is invited to visit Refs.
[
132
,
140
,
12
,
112
,
41
,
59
,
58
,
15
]
and in particular, the great book by Peter Olver
[
112
]
that includes pertinent historical remarks, reproducible examples, and crucial theorems for general differential equations. The theorem of interest for us is based on the concept of prolongation of a vector field and gives us the necessary and sufficient conditions to have a symmetry group. Without giving the details, it is stated as follows.

Theorem S27 (Theorem 2.71
[
112
]
).

Let
\Delta(x,u^{(n)})=0
be a nondegenerate system of
\ell
differential equations. A connected local group of transformations
G
acting on an open subset
M\subset X\times U
is a symmetry group of the system if and only if

\displaystyle\mathrm{pr}^{(n)}v[\Delta_{\nu}(x,u^{(n)})]=0\,,\quad\nu\in\{1,...,\ell\}\,,\quad\text{whenever}\quad\Delta(x,u^{(n)})=0\,,

(S89)

for every infinitesimal generator
v
of
G
.

Remark S28.

The function
\Delta
from the
n
-jet space
X\times U^{(n)}
to
\mathbb{R}^{\ell}
is considered to be smooth in its arguments
[
112
, p.96]
. Moreover, it is also assumed that the infinitesimal generator
v
and its prolongations act on the space of smooth functions, a fact that we will use later. Finally, we refer to p.20-22 of Ref.
[
112
]
for the definition of a connected local group of transformations.

In the next subsection, we use this general result for first-order ordinary differential equations and show that the infinitesimal criterion (
S89
) is elegantly written in terms of the Koopman generator.

S5.1
Proof of the Lemma: Infinitesimal criterion of symmetry under Koopman’s perspective

To use Thm.
S5
in our context, we first adapt Defs. 2.30 and 2.70 of Ref.
[
112
]
for systems of first-order ODEs.

Definition S29.
Let
\Delta_{i}(t,\boldsymbol{u},\dot{\boldsymbol{u}})=0
for
i\in\{1,...,N\}
be a system of first-order ordinary differential equations. The system is of maximal rank if the
N\times(2N+1)
Jacobian matrix of
\boldsymbol{\Delta}=(\Delta_{1},...,\Delta_{N})
,

\displaystyle J_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}(t,\boldsymbol{u},\dot{\boldsymbol{u}})=\begin{pmatrix}\frac{\partial\Delta_{1}(t,\boldsymbol{u},\dot{\boldsymbol{u}})}{\partial t}&\frac{\partial\Delta_{1}(t,\boldsymbol{u},\dot{\boldsymbol{u}})}{\partial u_{1}}&\cdots&\frac{\partial\Delta_{1}(t,\boldsymbol{u},\dot{\boldsymbol{u}})}{\partial u_{N}}&\frac{\partial\Delta_{1}(t,\boldsymbol{u},\dot{\boldsymbol{u}})}{\partial\dot{u}_{1}}&\cdots&\frac{\partial\Delta_{1}(t,\boldsymbol{u},\dot{\boldsymbol{u}})}{\partial\dot{u}_{N}}\\
\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\vdots\\
\frac{\partial\Delta_{N}(t,\boldsymbol{u},\dot{\boldsymbol{u}})}{\partial t}&\frac{\partial\Delta_{N}(t,\boldsymbol{u},\dot{\boldsymbol{u}})}{\partial u_{1}}&\cdots&\frac{\partial\Delta_{N}(t,\boldsymbol{u},\dot{\boldsymbol{u}})}{\partial u_{N}}&\frac{\partial\Delta_{N}(t,\boldsymbol{u},\dot{\boldsymbol{u}})}{\partial\dot{u}_{1}}&\cdots&\frac{\partial\Delta_{N}(t,\boldsymbol{u},\dot{\boldsymbol{u}})}{\partial\dot{u}_{N}}\end{pmatrix},

is of rank
N
for all
(t,\boldsymbol{u},\dot{\boldsymbol{u}})
such that
\boldsymbol{\Delta}(t,\boldsymbol{u},\dot{\boldsymbol{u}})=\boldsymbol{0}
.

Definition S30.
A system of
N
first-order differential equations,
\boldsymbol{\Delta}(t,\boldsymbol{u},\dot{\boldsymbol{u}})=0
is
locally solvable at the point

(t_{0},\boldsymbol{u}_{0},\dot{\boldsymbol{u}}_{0})\in\mathscr{G}_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}=\{(t,\boldsymbol{u},\dot{\boldsymbol{u}})\,|\,\boldsymbol{\Delta}(t,\boldsymbol{u},\dot{\boldsymbol{u}})=0\}
if there exists a smooth solution
\boldsymbol{u}=\boldsymbol{y}(t)
of the system, defined for
t
in a neighborhood of
t_{0}
, which has the prescribed “initial condition”
\mathrm{pr}^{(1)}\boldsymbol{y}(t_{0})=(\boldsymbol{u}_{0},\dot{\boldsymbol{u}}_{0})
. The system is
locally solvable
if it is locally solvable at every point of
\mathscr{G}_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}
. A system is
nondegenerate
if at every point
(t_{0},\boldsymbol{u}_{0},\dot{\boldsymbol{u}}_{0})\in\mathscr{G}_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}
it is both locally solvable and of maximal rank.

Consider the system of first-order ordinary differential equations (henceforth, called the “dynamics”)

\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}y_{i}}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t}=F_{i}(t,y_{1},...,y_{N})\,,\quad i\in\{1,...,N\}\,,

(S90)

with initial condition
y_{i}(t_{0})=(\boldsymbol{u}_{0})_{i}
for all
i
,
t_{0}<t
, and
F_{1},...,F_{N}
are smooth (
\mathscr{C}^{\infty}
) in their arguments. Note that we could relax the differentiability requirements in principle, but we use smooth functions for simplicity and to be coherent with the approach and the results in Ref.
[
112
, see p.4 and p.96]
. Let us define the smooth functions
\Delta_{i}
for all
i\in\{1,...,N\}
on the 1-jet space related to the
i
-th equation in the dynamics such that

\Delta_{i}(t,\boldsymbol{u},\dot{\boldsymbol{u}})=\dot{u}_{i}-F_{i}(t,\boldsymbol{u})\,,

(S91)

where
\boldsymbol{u}=(u_{1},...,u_{N})\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
and
\dot{\boldsymbol{u}}=(\dot{u}_{1},...,\dot{u}_{N})\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
are coordinates with
t\in\mathscr{T}
for the jet space. In such case, the next lemma shows that there is no problem with the system regarding the condition of maximal rank and local solvability.

Lemma S7.

The dynamics in Eq. (
S90
) is nondegenerate.

Proof.
The
N\times(2N+1)
Jacobian matrix of
\boldsymbol{\Delta}=(\Delta_{1},...,\Delta_{N})
where
\Delta_{i}
is given in Eq. (
S91
) is

\displaystyle J_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}(t,\boldsymbol{u},\dot{\boldsymbol{u}})=\begin{pmatrix}-\frac{\partial F_{1}(t,\boldsymbol{u})}{\partial t}&-\frac{\partial F_{1}(t,\boldsymbol{u})}{\partial u_{1}}&...&-\frac{\partial F_{1}(t,\boldsymbol{u})}{\partial u_{N}}&1&...&0\\
\vdots&\vdots&...&\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
-\frac{\partial F_{N}(t,\boldsymbol{u})}{\partial t}&-\frac{\partial F_{N}(t,\boldsymbol{u})}{\partial u_{1}}&...&-\frac{\partial F_{N}(t,\boldsymbol{u})}{\partial u_{N}}&0&...&1\end{pmatrix}

and is of rank
N
with respect to all
(t,\boldsymbol{u},\dot{\boldsymbol{u}})
, because of the
N\times N
identity submatrix in the last columns of
J_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}(t,\boldsymbol{u},\dot{\boldsymbol{u}})
. The dynamics is thus of maximal rank.

Moreover, by assumption, the vector field in Eq. (
S90
) is smooth. Thus, there exists a unique smooth solution
\boldsymbol{y}(t)=\boldsymbol{u}
starting at
\boldsymbol{y}(t_{0})=\boldsymbol{u}_{0}
by Lem. 2.3 of Ref.
[
142
]
. The existence of the solution and the form of the differential equations imply that
\dot{\boldsymbol{y}}(t_{0})=\boldsymbol{F}(t_{0},\boldsymbol{y}(t_{0}))=\boldsymbol{F}(t_{0},\boldsymbol{u}_{0})=:\dot{\boldsymbol{u}}_{0}
. Such solution exists for any initial condition
(\boldsymbol{u}_{0},\dot{\boldsymbol{u}}_{0})=\mathrm{pr}^{(1)}\boldsymbol{y}(t_{0})\in\mathscr{G}_{\Delta}
and the system is therefore locally solvable and altogether, nondegenerate.

Consider the set
\mathscr{O}
of time-dependent smooth observables
f:S\subset\mathscr{T}\times U\to\mathbb{R}
. The Koopman operator for a non-autonomous dynamical system described by Eq. (
S90
) is
U_{\varphi}:\mathscr{T}\times\mathscr{T}\times\mathscr{O}\to\mathscr{O}
with
U_{\varphi_{t_{0},t}}[f]:=U_{\varphi}(t_{0},t,f)
and it acts on an observable
f
such that

\displaystyle U_{\varphi_{t_{0},t}}[f]=f\circ\varphi_{t_{0},t}

with the properties
U_{\varphi_{t_{0},t_{0}}}=\mathrm{id}
and
U_{\varphi_{a,t+a}}\circ U_{\varphi_{t_{0},a}}=U_{\varphi_{t_{0},t+a}}

[
86
]
. There is a family
(\mathcal{U}_{t_{0}})_{t_{0}\in\mathscr{T}}
of Koopman generators

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{t_{0}}[f](\boldsymbol{u}_{0})=\left.\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}f(t,\boldsymbol{y}(t))}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t}\right|_{t=t_{0}}\,,

recalling that
\boldsymbol{y}(t_{0})=\boldsymbol{u}_{0}
and that
\mathcal{U}_{t}
is also locally defined at some point
\boldsymbol{u}\in U
. Performing the total derivative gives the explicit form

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{t_{0}}[f](\boldsymbol{u}_{0})=\left.\left[\frac{\partial f}{\partial t}(t,\boldsymbol{y}(t))+\boldsymbol{F}(t,\boldsymbol{y}(t))\cdot\nabla f(t,\boldsymbol{y}(t))\right]\right|_{t=t_{0}}=\frac{\partial f}{\partial t}(t_{0},\boldsymbol{u}_{0})+\boldsymbol{F}(t_{0},\boldsymbol{u}_{0})\cdot\nabla f(t_{0},\boldsymbol{u}_{0})\,.

Therefore, the Koopman generator is

\mathcal{U}=\partial_{t}+\sum_{j=1}^{N}F_{j}(t,\boldsymbol{u})\partial_{j}\,,

(S92)

where
(t,\boldsymbol{u})\in S
,
\partial_{t}:=\partial/\partial t
,
\partial_{j}:=\partial/\partial u_{j}
and where we have removed the time index of the generator for simplicity. Under these considerations, the Koopman generator
\mathcal{U}
and the infinitesimal generator
v
both act on smooth functions [Remark
S5
] and can be manipulated together. The next lemma is the equivalent of Thm.
S5
for systems of first-order ODEs and provides the infinitesimal criterion of symmetry in terms of the Koopman generator.

Lemma S8 (Lemma of the paper).

A connected local group of transformations
G
acting on an open subset
S\subset\mathscr{T}\times U
is a symmetry group of the dynamics in Eq. (
S90
) if and only if

\displaystyle[\mathcal{U},v]-\mathcal{U}[\xi(t,\boldsymbol{u})]\mathcal{U}=0

(S93)

for every infinitesimal generator
v=\xi(t,\boldsymbol{u})\partial_{t}+\sum_{j=1}^{N}\phi_{j}(t,\boldsymbol{u})\partial_{j}
of
G
.

Proof.
To begin with, Lem.
S5.1
ensures that the dynamics (
S90
) is nondegenerate, which guarantees that Thm.
S5
can be applied. Now, since the dynamics is a first-order system of ODEs, only the first prolongation of the infinitesimal generator
v
is needed. By the general prolongation formula in Ref.
[
112
, Theorem 2.36]
, the first prolongation of
v
is

\mathrm{pr}^{(1)}v=v+\sum_{j=1}^{N}\phi_{j}^{t}(t,\boldsymbol{u},\dot{\boldsymbol{u}})\frac{\partial}{\partial\dot{u}_{j}}\,,

with

\phi^{t}_{j}=D_{t}(\phi_{j}-\xi\dot{u}_{j})+\xi\ddot{u}_{j}=\dot{\phi}_{j}+\sum_{k=1}^{N}(\partial_{k}\phi_{j}-\dot{\xi}\delta_{jk})F_{k}-\sum_{k=1}^{N}\partial_{k}\xi\,F_{k}F_{j}\,,

(S94)

where the superscript
t
in
\phi^{t}_{j}
is a label used to denote the component of the prolongation associated with
\partial/\partial\dot{u}_{j}
and
D_{t}=d/dt
is the total derivative. The dependencies on
t,\boldsymbol{u},\dot{\boldsymbol{u}}
are omitted in Eq. (
S94
) to simplify the notation, as will be done from now on. The infinitesimal condition for
G
to be a symmetry group is then

\displaystyle\mathrm{pr}^{(1)}v[\Delta_{i}]

\displaystyle=\xi\dot{\Delta}_{i}+\sum_{j=1}^{N}\phi_{j}\partial_{j}\Delta_{i}+\sum_{j=1}^{N}\phi^{t}_{j}\frac{\partial\Delta_{i}}{\partial\dot{u}_{j}}=0\,,

for all
i\in\{1,...,N\}
. Inserting Eq. (
S94
), performing the derivatives and rearranging leads to the infinitesimal criterion

\dot{\phi}_{i}+\sum_{j=1}^{N}(\partial_{j}\phi_{i}-\dot{\xi}\delta_{ij})F_{j}-\sum_{j=1}^{N}\partial_{j}\xi F_{j}F_{i}=\xi\dot{F}_{i}+\sum_{j=1}^{N}\phi_{j}\partial_{j}F_{i}\,,\quad\forall i\in\{1,...,N\}\,.

(S95)

Applying Thm.
S5
to our particular case, a connected local group of transformations
G
is a symmetry group if and only if Eqs. (
S95
) are satisfied. It now remains to show that Eqs. (
S95
) are equivalent to condition (
S93
). The commutator of
\mathcal{U}
and
v
is

\displaystyle[\mathcal{U},v]

\displaystyle=[\partial_{t}+\sum_{j}F_{j}\partial_{j},\xi\partial_{t}+\sum_{i}\phi_{i}\partial_{i}]=[\partial_{t},\xi\partial_{t}]+\sum_{i}[\partial_{t},\phi_{i}\partial_{i}]+\sum_{j}[F_{j}\partial_{j},\xi\partial_{t}]+\sum_{i,j}[F_{j}\partial_{j},\phi_{i}\partial_{i}]\,,

where we have used the bilinearity of the commutator. More explicitly, the last equation is

\displaystyle[\mathcal{U},v]

\displaystyle=(\partial_{t}\xi)\partial_{t}+\sum_{i}(\partial_{t}\phi_{i})\partial_{i}+\sum_{j}(F_{j}(\partial_{j}\xi)\partial_{t}-\xi(\partial_{t}F_{j})\partial_{j})+\sum_{i,j}(F_{j}(\partial_{j}\phi_{i})\partial_{i}-\phi_{i}(\partial_{i}F_{j})\partial_{j})\,,

henceforth considering that all sums run from 1 to
N
. Given some observable
f\in\mathscr{O}
, the expression can be rearranged to yield

\displaystyle[\mathcal{U},v]f

\displaystyle=\mathcal{U}[\xi]\partial_{t}f+\sum_{i}(\dot{\phi}_{i}+\sum_{j}\partial_{j}\phi_{i}\,F_{j}-\xi\dot{F}_{i}-\sum_{j}\phi_{j}\partial_{j}F_{i})\partial_{i}f\,.

From the definition of the Koopman generator (
S92
), the relation
\partial_{t}f=\mathcal{U}[f]-\sum_{i}F_{i}\partial_{i}f
holds and implies

\displaystyle[\mathcal{U},v]f=\mathcal{U}[\xi]\mathcal{U}[f]+\sum_{i}(\dot{\phi}_{i}-\partial_{t}\xi\,F_{i}+\sum_{j}\partial_{j}\phi_{i}F_{j}-\sum_{j}F_{i}F_{j}\partial_{j}\xi-\xi\dot{F}_{i}-\sum_{j}\phi_{j}\partial_{j}F_{i})\partial_{i}f\,.

Writing
\partial_{t}\xi\,F_{i}
as
\sum_{j}(\dot{\xi}\delta_{ij})F_{j}
explicitly gives a sum over the
N
equations of the infinitesimal symmetry condition:

\displaystyle[\mathcal{U},v]=\mathcal{U}[\xi]\mathcal{U}+\sum_{i}(\dot{\phi}_{i}+\sum_{j}(\partial_{j}\phi_{i}-\dot{\xi}\delta_{ij})F_{j}-\sum_{j}\partial_{j}\xi F_{j}F_{i}-\xi\dot{F}_{i}-\sum_{j}\phi_{j}\partial_{j}F_{i})\partial_{i}\,.

On the one hand, if the
N
infinitesimal conditions of symmetry in Eqs. (
S95
) are satisfied, then

\displaystyle[\mathcal{U},v]-\mathcal{U}[\xi]\mathcal{U}=0\,.

On the other hand, if
[\mathcal{U},v]-\mathcal{U}[\xi]\mathcal{U}=0
, then

\displaystyle\sum_{i}(\dot{\phi}_{i}+\sum_{j}(\partial_{j}\phi_{i}-\dot{\xi}\delta_{ij})F_{j}-\sum_{j}\partial_{j}\xi F_{j}F_{i}-\xi\dot{F}_{i}-\sum_{j}\phi_{j}\partial_{j}F_{i})\partial_{i}=0\,.

But each term of the sum over
i
is independent, meaning that Eqs. (
S95
) are satisfied and thus completing the proof.

S5.2
Basic symmetries of the Kuramoto model

The Koopman generator of the Kuramoto dynamics on functions of time and
\boldsymbol{z}\in\mathbb{T}^{N}
is

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}+\sum_{j,k=1}^{N}(A_{jk}z_{k}-\bar{A}_{jk}\bar{z}_{k}z_{j}^{2})\partial_{j}=\partial_{t}+\mathcal{K}\,.

The most general infinitesimal generator of (potential) symmetries is

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}=\xi(t,z)\partial_{t}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{N}\phi_{\ell}(t,z)\partial_{\ell}\,.

(S96)

Adapting Lem.
S5.1
for coordinates of time and the
N
-torus, it is necessary and sufficient that the generator
\mathcal{S}
satisfies

\displaystyle[\mathcal{U},\mathcal{S}]-\mathcal{U}[\xi(t,z)]\,\mathcal{U}=0\,.

(S97)

It is easy to verify that

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{1}=iL_{0}\,,\qquad\mathcal{S}_{2}=\mathcal{K}\,,\qquad\mathcal{S}_{3}=f(t)\mathcal{U}

are Lie symmetries of the Kuramoto dynamics. Note that the time translation generator
\partial_{t}
is obtained with
\mathcal{U}-\mathcal{K}
and is a symmetry generator, as expected of any autonomous dynamical system. In fact, denoting
\mathcal{S}=\mathcal{S}_{\xi}+\mathcal{S}_{\phi}
with
\mathcal{S}_{\xi}=\xi(t,\boldsymbol{z})\partial_{t}
and
\mathcal{S}_{\phi}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{N}\phi_{\ell}(t,\boldsymbol{z})\partial_{\ell}
, the infinitesimal criterion for the Kuramoto model becomes
[\mathcal{U},\mathcal{S}_{\phi}]-\mathcal{U}[\xi(t,\boldsymbol{z})]\,\mathcal{K}=0
, that is, the form of the condition for autonomous dynamical systems.

On the one hand, if
\phi_{1}(t,\boldsymbol{z}),...,\phi_{N}(t,\boldsymbol{z})
are zero, the condition becomes

\mathcal{U}[\xi(t,\boldsymbol{z})]=0
, meaning that
\xi(t,\boldsymbol{z})
must be a constant of motion if
\xi(t,z)\partial_{t}
is to be a generator of symmetry (also highlighted in Ref.
[
138
]
). Yet, such symmetries simply act as time translations. Indeed, considering that the conditions of Thm.
S4.4
are satisfied for some quadruples of vertices, one can set
\xi(t,z)
to be any of the conserved functionally independent cross-ratios, leading to the infinitesimal symmetry generators
\mathcal{S}_{abcd}=c_{abcd}(\boldsymbol{z})\partial_{t}
for all
a,b,c,d
such that
\mathcal{U}[c_{abcd}(\boldsymbol{z})]=\mathcal{K}[c_{abcd}(\boldsymbol{z})]=0
. The action of the symmetry group on the coordinates is thus
e^{\epsilon\mathcal{S}_{abcd}}t=t+\epsilon\,c_{abcd}(\boldsymbol{z})
and
e^{\epsilon\mathcal{S}_{abcd}}z_{j}=z_{j}
. Considering that
a,b,c,d
belong to some partially integrable part
P
, using
z_{j}(t)=M_{t}(w_{j})
(notation of Ref.
[
88
]
) and the fact that cross-ratios are invariant under Möbius transformations
M_{t}
leads to

\displaystyle c_{abcd}(\boldsymbol{z}(t))=(M_{t}(w_{a}),M_{t}(w_{b});M_{t}(w_{c}),M_{t}(w_{d}))=(w_{a},w_{b}\,;\,w_{c},w_{d})=c_{abcd}(\boldsymbol{w})

and the action of the related symmetry group on a solution
\boldsymbol{z}(t)
of the Kuramoto model is such that

\displaystyle\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}(t):=e^{\epsilon\mathcal{S}_{abcd}}\boldsymbol{z}(t)=\boldsymbol{z}(t+\epsilon\,c_{abcd}(\boldsymbol{w}))\,,

where
\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}(t)
is an analogous time-translated solution of the Kuramoto model.

On the other hand, if
\xi(t,\boldsymbol{z})=0
, then the infinitesimal criterion (
S93
) is
[\mathcal{U},\mathcal{S}]=0
. For
\mathcal{S}=\psi(t,\boldsymbol{z})\tilde{\mathcal{S}}
with some smooth function
\psi
and
\tilde{\mathcal{S}}=\sum_{j=1}^{N}\tilde{\phi}_{j}(t,\boldsymbol{z})\partial_{j}
,
[\mathcal{U},\mathcal{S}]=\mathcal{U}[\psi(t,\boldsymbol{z})]\tilde{\mathcal{S}}+\psi(t,\boldsymbol{z})[\mathcal{U},\tilde{\mathcal{S}}]
. Therefore, if
\psi(\boldsymbol{z})
is a constant of motion and
\tilde{\mathcal{S}}
is a symmetry generator, then
\psi(\boldsymbol{z})\tilde{\mathcal{S}}
is also a symmetry generator, but its action remains the one of
\tilde{\mathcal{S}}
or is not an automorphism of the
N
-torus, making it of little use.

We will need more than naive inspection to uncover additional symmetries. Therefore, the next two subsections are dedicated to deriving the determining equations and developing a method for obtaining particular solutions.

S5.3
General determining equations for the Kuramoto model

To obtain the determining equations, it is useful to introduce basic commutation relations. This is the purpose of the next lemma.

Lemma S9.

Consider the elements of the vectorial Euler differential operators defined in Eq. (
S13
) and let
\mathcal{K}
be defined by Eq. (
S14
). Then, the following commutation relations hold for all
j,k\in\{1,...,N\}
and
m,n\in\mathbb{Z}
:

[\,\ell_{j}^{m},\,\ell_{k}^{n}\,]=\delta_{jk}(n-m)\,\ell_{j}^{m+n}\,,\qquad[\,z_{j}^{m},\,z_{k}^{n}\,]=0\,,\qquad[\,\ell_{j}^{m},\,z_{k}^{n}\,]=\delta_{jk}\,n\,z_{k}^{m+n}

(S98)

and

\displaystyle[\,\mathcal{K},\,z_{j}^{n}\,]

\displaystyle=nz_{j}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\left(A_{jk}z_{k}z_{j}^{-1}-\bar{A}_{jk}z_{k}^{-1}z_{j}^{1}\right)=2inz_{j}^{n}\Imag\left(\sum_{k=1}^{N}A_{jk}z_{k}z_{j}^{-1}\right)\,,

(S99)

\displaystyle[\,\mathcal{K},\,\ell_{j}^{n}\,]

\displaystyle=(n+1)\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{N}A_{jk}z_{k}\Big)\ell_{j}^{n-1}-(n-1)\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{N}\bar{A}_{jk}z_{k}^{-1}\Big)\ell_{j}^{n+1}-z_{j}^{n+1}\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{N}A_{kj}\ell_{k}^{-1}\Big)-z_{j}^{n-1}\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{N}\bar{A}_{kj}\ell_{k}^{1}\Big)\,.

(S100)

Proof.
The commutation relations in Eq. (
S98
) are obtained easily from the definition in Eq. (
S13
). Then,

\displaystyle[\,\mathcal{K},\,z_{j}^{n}\,]

\displaystyle=\sum_{q,k}A_{qk}[z_{k}\ell_{q}^{-1},z_{j}^{n}]-\sum_{q,k}\bar{A}_{qk}[z_{k}^{-1}\ell^{1}_{q},z_{j}^{n}]

\displaystyle[\,\mathcal{K},\,\ell_{j}^{n}\,]

\displaystyle=\sum_{q,k}A_{qk}[z_{k}\ell_{q}^{-1},\ell_{j}^{n}]-\sum_{q,k}\bar{A}_{qk}[z_{k}^{-1}\ell^{1}_{q},\ell_{j}^{n}]\,.

Using the linearity of the commutator, the general formula
[AB,C]=A[B,C]+[A,C]B
and Eq. (
S98
) readily provides

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &=\delta_{qj}nz_{k}z_{j}^{n-1}\\
[z_{k}^{-1}\ell_{q}^{1},z_{j}^{n}]&=\delta_{qj}nz_{k}^{-1}z_{j}^{n+1}\end{aligned}\qquad\begin{aligned} &=\delta_{qj}(n+1)z_{k}\ell_{q}^{n-1}-\delta_{jk}z_{k}^{n+1}\ell_{q}^{-1}\\
[z_{k}^{-1}\ell_{q}^{1},\ell_{j}^{n}]&=\delta_{qj}(n-1)z_{k}^{-1}\ell_{q}^{n+1}+\delta_{jk}z_{k}^{n-1}\ell_{q}^{1}\end{aligned}

and their substitution yields the desired results

\displaystyle[\,\mathcal{K},\,z_{j}^{n}\,]

\displaystyle=\sum_{q,k}A_{qk}\delta_{qj}nz_{k}z_{j}^{n-1}-\sum_{q,k}\bar{A}_{qk}\delta_{qj}nz_{k}^{-1}z_{j}^{n+1}=n\sum_{k}A_{jk}z_{k}z_{j}^{n-1}-n\sum_{k}\bar{A}_{jk}z_{k}^{-1}z_{j}^{n+1}

\displaystyle[\,\mathcal{K},\,\ell_{j}^{n}\,]

\displaystyle=\sum_{q,k}A_{qk}(\delta_{qj}(n+1)z_{k}\ell_{q}^{n-1}-\delta_{jk}z_{k}^{n+1}\ell_{q}^{-1})-\sum_{q,k}\bar{A}_{qk}(\delta_{qj}(n-1)z_{k}^{-1}\ell_{q}^{n+1}+\delta_{jk}z_{k}^{n-1}\ell_{q}^{1})

\displaystyle=(n+1)\Big(\sum_{k}A_{jk}z_{k}\Big)\ell_{j}^{n-1}-z_{j}^{n+1}\Big(\sum_{q}A_{qj}\ell_{q}^{-1}\Big)-(n-1)\Big(\sum_{k}\bar{A}_{jk}z_{k}^{-1}\Big)\ell_{j}^{n+1}-z_{j}^{n-1}\Big(\sum_{q}\bar{A}_{qj}\ell_{q}^{1}\Big)

\displaystyle=(n+1)\Big(\sum_{k}A_{jk}z_{k}\Big)\ell_{j}^{n-1}-(n-1)\Big(\sum_{k}\bar{A}_{jk}z_{k}^{-1}\Big)\ell_{j}^{n+1}-z_{j}^{n+1}\Big(\sum_{k}A_{kj}\ell_{k}^{-1}\Big)-z_{j}^{n-1}\Big(\sum_{k}\bar{A}_{kj}\ell_{k}^{1}\Big)\,.

As mentioned in the main text, it is also useful to simplify calculations to restrict the general symmetry generator
\mathcal{S}
to one where
\xi
and
\phi_{1},...,\phi_{N}
are periodic functions, allowing us to expand them in Fourier series:

\displaystyle\xi(t,z)=\sum_{\boldsymbol{p}\in\mathbb{Z}^{N}}\varepsilon_{\boldsymbol{p}}(t)z^{\boldsymbol{p}}\,,\qquad\phi_{\ell}(t,z)=\sum_{\boldsymbol{p}\in\mathbb{Z}^{N}}\varphi_{\ell\boldsymbol{p}}(t)z^{\boldsymbol{p}}\,,\qquad\text{with}\quad z^{\boldsymbol{p}}=\prod_{j=1}^{N}z_{j}^{p_{j}}\,.

This assumption and some notation simplifications lead to

\displaystyle[\mathcal{U},\mathcal{S}]-\mathcal{U}[\xi(t,z)]\,\mathcal{U}=\sum_{\boldsymbol{p}}\Big[\mathcal{U},\varepsilon_{\boldsymbol{p}}(t)z^{\boldsymbol{p}}\partial_{t}\Big]+\sum_{\ell,\boldsymbol{p}}\Big[\mathcal{U},\varphi_{\ell\boldsymbol{p}}(t)z^{\boldsymbol{p}}\partial_{\ell}\Big]-\Big(\sum_{\boldsymbol{p}}\mathcal{U}\Big[\varepsilon_{\boldsymbol{p}}(t)z^{\boldsymbol{p}}\Big]\Big)\,\mathcal{U}\,.

After some manipulations using Lem.
S5.3
and simplifications, one finds

\displaystyle[\mathcal{U},\mathcal{S}]-\mathcal{U}[\xi(t,z)]\,\mathcal{U}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell,\boldsymbol{p}}\dot{\varphi}_{\ell\boldsymbol{p}}(t)z^{\boldsymbol{p}}\partial_{\ell}+\sum_{\ell,\boldsymbol{p},j,k}\varphi_{\ell\boldsymbol{p}}(t)(A_{jk}[z_{k}\partial_{j},z^{\boldsymbol{p}}\partial_{\ell}]-\bar{A}_{jk}[z_{k}^{-1}z_{j}^{2}\partial_{j},z^{\boldsymbol{p}}\partial_{\ell}])

\displaystyle-\sum_{\boldsymbol{p}}\left(\dot{\varepsilon}_{\boldsymbol{p}}(t)+\varepsilon_{\boldsymbol{p}}(t)\sum_{r,s}p_{r}(A_{rs}z_{r}^{-1}z_{s}-\bar{A}_{rs}z_{r}z_{s}^{-1})\right)z^{\boldsymbol{p}}\sum_{j,k}(A_{jk}z_{k}-\bar{A}_{jk}\bar{z}_{k}z_{j}^{2})\partial_{j}\,.

The commutation relations are explicitly given by

\displaystyle[z_{k}\partial_{j},z^{\boldsymbol{p}}\partial_{\ell}]

\displaystyle=p_{j}z^{\boldsymbol{p}-\boldsymbol{e}_{j}+\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}\partial_{\ell}-\delta_{k\ell}z^{\boldsymbol{p}}\partial_{j}

\displaystyle[z_{k}^{-1}z_{j}^{2}\partial_{j},z^{\boldsymbol{p}}\partial_{\ell}]

\displaystyle=p_{j}z^{\boldsymbol{p}+\boldsymbol{e}_{j}-\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}\partial_{\ell}+\delta_{k\ell}z^{\boldsymbol{p}+2\boldsymbol{e}_{j}-2\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}\partial_{j}-2z^{\boldsymbol{p}+\boldsymbol{e}_{j}-\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}\delta_{j\ell}\partial_{j}\,.

Substituting these commutation relations into the infinitesimal condition yields, after simplifications,

\displaystyle[\mathcal{U},\mathcal{S}]-\mathcal{U}[\xi(t,z)]\,\mathcal{U}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell,\boldsymbol{p}}\dot{\varphi}_{\ell\boldsymbol{p}}(t)z^{\boldsymbol{p}}\partial_{\ell}+\sum_{\ell,\boldsymbol{p},j,k}\varphi_{\ell\boldsymbol{p}}(t)A_{jk}p_{j}z^{\boldsymbol{p}-\boldsymbol{e}_{j}+\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}\partial_{\ell}-\sum_{\ell,\boldsymbol{p},j}\varphi_{\ell\boldsymbol{p}}(t)A_{j\ell}z^{\boldsymbol{p}}\partial_{j}

\displaystyle-\sum_{\ell,\boldsymbol{p},j,k}\varphi_{\ell\boldsymbol{p}}(t)\bar{A}_{jk}p_{j}z^{\boldsymbol{p}+\boldsymbol{e}_{j}-\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}\partial_{\ell}-\sum_{\ell,\boldsymbol{p},j}\varphi_{\ell\boldsymbol{p}}(t)\bar{A}_{j\ell}z^{\boldsymbol{p}+2\boldsymbol{e}_{j}-2\boldsymbol{e}_{\ell}}\partial_{j}+2\sum_{\ell,\boldsymbol{p},k}\varphi_{\ell\boldsymbol{p}}(t)\bar{A}_{\ell k}z^{\boldsymbol{p}+\boldsymbol{e}_{\ell}-\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}\partial_{\ell}

\displaystyle-\sum_{\boldsymbol{p}}\left(\dot{\varepsilon}_{\boldsymbol{p}}(t)+\varepsilon_{\boldsymbol{p}}(t)\sum_{r,s}p_{r}(A_{rs}z_{r}^{-1}z_{s}-\bar{A}_{rs}z_{r}z_{s}^{-1})\right)z^{\boldsymbol{p}}\sum_{j,k}(A_{jk}z_{k}-\bar{A}_{jk}\bar{z}_{k}z_{j}^{2})\partial_{j}\,.

From there, let’s simplify again the equations to extract the determining equations. First,

\displaystyle[\mathcal{U},\mathcal{S}]-\mathcal{U}[\xi(t,z)]\,\mathcal{U}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell,\boldsymbol{p}}\dot{\varphi}_{\ell\boldsymbol{p}}(t)z^{\boldsymbol{p}}\partial_{\ell}+\sum_{\ell,\boldsymbol{p},j,k}\varphi_{\ell\boldsymbol{p}}(t)A_{jk}p_{j}z^{\boldsymbol{p}-\boldsymbol{e}_{j}+\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}\partial_{\ell}-\sum_{\ell,\boldsymbol{p},j}\varphi_{\ell\boldsymbol{p}}(t)A_{j\ell}z^{\boldsymbol{p}}\partial_{j}

\displaystyle-\sum_{\ell,\boldsymbol{p},j,k}\varphi_{\ell\boldsymbol{p}}(t)\bar{A}_{jk}p_{j}z^{\boldsymbol{p}+\boldsymbol{e}_{j}-\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}\partial_{\ell}-\sum_{\ell,\boldsymbol{p},j}\varphi_{\ell\boldsymbol{p}}(t)\bar{A}_{j\ell}z^{\boldsymbol{p}+2\boldsymbol{e}_{j}-2\boldsymbol{e}_{\ell}}\partial_{j}+2\sum_{\ell,\boldsymbol{p},k}\varphi_{\ell\boldsymbol{p}}(t)\bar{A}_{\ell k}z^{\boldsymbol{p}+\boldsymbol{e}_{\ell}-\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}\partial_{\ell}

\displaystyle-\sum_{\boldsymbol{p},j,k}\dot{\varepsilon}_{\boldsymbol{p}}(t)A_{jk}z^{\boldsymbol{p}+\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}\partial_{j}+\sum_{\boldsymbol{p},j,k}\dot{\varepsilon}_{\boldsymbol{p}}(t)\bar{A}_{jk}z^{\boldsymbol{p}+2\boldsymbol{e}_{j}-\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}\partial_{j}

\displaystyle-\sum_{\boldsymbol{p},j,k,r,s}p_{r}\varepsilon_{\boldsymbol{p}}(t)A_{rs}A_{jk}z^{\boldsymbol{p}-\boldsymbol{e}_{r}+\boldsymbol{e}_{s}+\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}\partial_{j}+\sum_{\boldsymbol{p},j,k,r,s}p_{r}\varepsilon_{\boldsymbol{p}}(t)A_{rs}\bar{A}_{jk}z^{\boldsymbol{p}-\boldsymbol{e}_{r}+\boldsymbol{e}_{s}-\boldsymbol{e}_{k}+2\boldsymbol{e}_{j}}\partial_{j}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\boldsymbol{p},j,k,r,s}p_{r}\varepsilon_{\boldsymbol{p}}(t)\bar{A}_{rs}A_{jk}z^{\boldsymbol{p}+\boldsymbol{e}_{r}-\boldsymbol{e}_{s}+\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}\partial_{j}+\sum_{\boldsymbol{p},j,k,r,s}p_{r}\varepsilon_{\boldsymbol{p}}(t)\bar{A}_{rs}\bar{A}_{jk}z^{\boldsymbol{p}+\boldsymbol{e}_{r}-\boldsymbol{e}_{s}-\boldsymbol{e}_{k}+2\boldsymbol{e}_{j}}\partial_{j}\,.

Making the change of indices to yield
z^{\boldsymbol{p}}
in every term leads to

\displaystyle[\mathcal{U},\mathcal{S}]-\mathcal{U}[\xi(t,z)]\,\mathcal{U}=\sum_{\ell,\boldsymbol{p}}\dot{\varphi}_{\ell\boldsymbol{p}}(t)z^{\boldsymbol{p}}\partial_{\ell}+\sum_{\ell,\boldsymbol{p},j,k}(p_{j}+1-\delta_{jk})\varphi_{\ell\boldsymbol{p}+\boldsymbol{e}_{j}-\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}(t)A_{jk}z^{\boldsymbol{p}}\partial_{\ell}-\sum_{\ell,\boldsymbol{p},j}\varphi_{\ell\boldsymbol{p}}(t)A_{j\ell}z^{\boldsymbol{p}}\partial_{j}

\displaystyle-\sum_{\ell,\boldsymbol{p},j,k}(p_{j}-1+\delta_{jk})\varphi_{\ell\boldsymbol{p}-\boldsymbol{e}_{j}+\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}(t)\bar{A}_{jk}z^{\boldsymbol{p}}\partial_{\ell}-\sum_{\ell,\boldsymbol{p},j}\varphi_{\ell\boldsymbol{p}-2\boldsymbol{e}_{j}+2\boldsymbol{e}_{\ell}}(t)\bar{A}_{j\ell}z^{\boldsymbol{p}}\partial_{j}+2\sum_{\ell,\boldsymbol{p},k}\varphi_{\ell\boldsymbol{p}-\boldsymbol{e}_{\ell}+\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}(t)\bar{A}_{\ell k}z^{\boldsymbol{p}}\partial_{\ell}

\displaystyle-\sum_{\boldsymbol{p},j,k}\dot{\varepsilon}_{\boldsymbol{p}-\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}(t)A_{jk}z^{\boldsymbol{p}}\partial_{j}+\sum_{\boldsymbol{p},j,k}\dot{\varepsilon}_{\boldsymbol{p}-2\boldsymbol{e}_{j}+\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}(t)\bar{A}_{jk}z^{\boldsymbol{p}}\partial_{j}-\sum_{\boldsymbol{p},j,k,r,s}(p_{r}+1-\delta_{rs}-\delta_{rk})\varepsilon_{\boldsymbol{p}+\boldsymbol{e}_{r}-\boldsymbol{e}_{s}-\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}(t)A_{rs}A_{jk}z^{\boldsymbol{p}}\partial_{j}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\boldsymbol{p},j,k,r,s}(p_{r}+1-\delta_{rs}+\delta_{rk}-2\delta_{rj})\varepsilon_{\boldsymbol{p}+\boldsymbol{e}_{r}-\boldsymbol{e}_{s}+\boldsymbol{e}_{k}-2\boldsymbol{e}_{j}}(t)A_{rs}\bar{A}_{jk}z^{\boldsymbol{p}}\partial_{j}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\boldsymbol{p},j,k,r,s}(p_{r}-1+\delta_{rs}-\delta_{rk})\varepsilon_{\boldsymbol{p}-\boldsymbol{e}_{r}+\boldsymbol{e}_{s}-\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}(t)\bar{A}_{rs}A_{jk}z^{\boldsymbol{p}}\partial_{j}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\boldsymbol{p},j,k,r,s}(p_{r}-1+\delta_{rs}+\delta_{rk}-2\delta_{rj})\varepsilon_{\boldsymbol{p}-\boldsymbol{e}_{r}+\boldsymbol{e}_{s}+\boldsymbol{e}_{k}-2\boldsymbol{e}_{j}}(t)\bar{A}_{rs}\bar{A}_{jk}z^{\boldsymbol{p}}\partial_{j}\,.

The change of indices from
j
to
\ell
to get a factor
\partial_{\ell}
in every sum implies

\displaystyle[\mathcal{U},\mathcal{S}]-\mathcal{U}[\xi(t,z)]\,\mathcal{U}=\sum_{\ell,\boldsymbol{p}}\dot{\varphi}_{\ell\boldsymbol{p}}(t)z^{\boldsymbol{p}}\partial_{\ell}+\sum_{\ell,\boldsymbol{p},j,k}(p_{j}+1-\delta_{jk})\varphi_{\ell\boldsymbol{p}+\boldsymbol{e}_{j}-\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}(t)A_{jk}z^{\boldsymbol{p}}\partial_{\ell}-\sum_{j,\boldsymbol{p},\ell}\varphi_{j\boldsymbol{p}}(t)A_{\ell j}z^{\boldsymbol{p}}\partial_{\ell}

\displaystyle-\sum_{\ell,\boldsymbol{p},j,k}(p_{j}-1+\delta_{jk})\varphi_{\ell\boldsymbol{p}-\boldsymbol{e}_{j}+\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}(t)\bar{A}_{jk}z^{\boldsymbol{p}}\partial_{\ell}-\sum_{j,\boldsymbol{p},\ell}\varphi_{j\boldsymbol{p}-2\boldsymbol{e}_{\ell}+2\boldsymbol{e}_{j}}(t)\bar{A}_{\ell j}z^{\boldsymbol{p}}\partial_{\ell}+2\sum_{\ell,\boldsymbol{p},k}\varphi_{\ell\boldsymbol{p}-\boldsymbol{e}_{\ell}+\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}(t)\bar{A}_{\ell k}z^{\boldsymbol{p}}\partial_{\ell}

\displaystyle-\sum_{\boldsymbol{p},\ell,k}\dot{\varepsilon}_{\boldsymbol{p}-\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}(t)A_{\ell k}z^{\boldsymbol{p}}\partial_{\ell}+\sum_{\boldsymbol{p},\ell,k}\dot{\varepsilon}_{\boldsymbol{p}-2\boldsymbol{e}_{\ell}+\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}(t)\bar{A}_{\ell k}z^{\boldsymbol{p}}\partial_{\ell}-\sum_{\boldsymbol{p},\ell,k,r,s}(p_{r}+1-\delta_{rs}-\delta_{rk})\varepsilon_{\boldsymbol{p}+\boldsymbol{e}_{r}-\boldsymbol{e}_{s}-\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}(t)A_{rs}A_{\ell k}z^{\boldsymbol{p}}\partial_{\ell}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\boldsymbol{p},\ell,k,r,s}(p_{r}+1-\delta_{rs}+\delta_{rk}-2\delta_{r\ell})\varepsilon_{\boldsymbol{p}+\boldsymbol{e}_{r}-\boldsymbol{e}_{s}+\boldsymbol{e}_{k}-2\boldsymbol{e}_{\ell}}(t)A_{rs}\bar{A}_{\ell k}z^{\boldsymbol{p}}\partial_{\ell}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\boldsymbol{p},\ell,k,r,s}(p_{r}-1+\delta_{rs}-\delta_{rk})\varepsilon_{\boldsymbol{p}-\boldsymbol{e}_{r}+\boldsymbol{e}_{s}-\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}(t)\bar{A}_{rs}A_{\ell k}z^{\boldsymbol{p}}\partial_{\ell}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\boldsymbol{p},\ell,k,r,s}(p_{r}-1+\delta_{rs}+\delta_{rk}-2\delta_{r\ell})\varepsilon_{\boldsymbol{p}-\boldsymbol{e}_{r}+\boldsymbol{e}_{s}+\boldsymbol{e}_{k}-2\boldsymbol{e}_{\ell}}(t)\bar{A}_{rs}\bar{A}_{\ell k}z^{\boldsymbol{p}}\partial_{\ell}\,.

The infinitesimal condition of symmetry then yields

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\dot{\varphi}_{\ell\boldsymbol{p}}(t)+\sum_{j,k}(p_{j}+1-\delta_{jk})A_{jk}\varphi_{\ell\boldsymbol{p}+\boldsymbol{e}_{j}-\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}(t)-\sum_{j}A_{\ell j}\varphi_{j\boldsymbol{p}}(t)-\sum_{j,k}(p_{j}-1+\delta_{jk})\bar{A}_{jk}\varphi_{\ell\boldsymbol{p}-\boldsymbol{e}_{j}+\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}(t)

\displaystyle-\sum_{j}\bar{A}_{\ell j}\varphi_{j\boldsymbol{p}-2\boldsymbol{e}_{\ell}+2\boldsymbol{e}_{j}}(t)+2\sum_{k}\bar{A}_{\ell k}\varphi_{\ell\boldsymbol{p}-\boldsymbol{e}_{\ell}+\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}(t)-\sum_{k}A_{\ell k}\dot{\varepsilon}_{\boldsymbol{p}-\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}(t)+\sum_{k}\bar{A}_{\ell k}\dot{\varepsilon}_{\boldsymbol{p}-2\boldsymbol{e}_{\ell}+\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}(t)

\displaystyle-\sum_{k,r,s}(p_{r}+1-\delta_{rs}-\delta_{rk})A_{rs}A_{\ell k}\varepsilon_{\boldsymbol{p}+\boldsymbol{e}_{r}-\boldsymbol{e}_{s}-\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}(t)+\sum_{k,r,s}(p_{r}+1-\delta_{rs}+\delta_{rk}-2\delta_{r\ell})A_{rs}\bar{A}_{\ell k}\varepsilon_{\boldsymbol{p}+\boldsymbol{e}_{r}-\boldsymbol{e}_{s}+\boldsymbol{e}_{k}-2\boldsymbol{e}_{\ell}}(t)

\displaystyle+\sum_{k,r,s}(p_{r}-1+\delta_{rs}-\delta_{rk})\bar{A}_{rs}A_{\ell k}\varepsilon_{\boldsymbol{p}-\boldsymbol{e}_{r}+\boldsymbol{e}_{s}-\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}(t)+\sum_{k,r,s}(p_{r}-1+\delta_{rs}+\delta_{rk}-2\delta_{r\ell})\bar{A}_{rs}\bar{A}_{\ell k}\varepsilon_{\boldsymbol{p}-\boldsymbol{e}_{r}+\boldsymbol{e}_{s}+\boldsymbol{e}_{k}-2\boldsymbol{e}_{\ell}}(t)

for all
\ell\in\{1,...,N\}
,
\boldsymbol{p}\in\mathbb{Z}^{N}
. By rearranging, one finds the general determining equations

\displaystyle\dot{\varphi}_{\ell\boldsymbol{p}}(t)-\sum_{k}A_{\ell k}\dot{\varepsilon}_{\boldsymbol{p}-\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}(t)+\sum_{k}\bar{A}_{\ell k}\dot{\varepsilon}_{\boldsymbol{p}-2\boldsymbol{e}_{\ell}+\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}(t)=\sum_{k}[A_{\ell k}\varphi_{k\boldsymbol{p}}(t)+\bar{A}_{\ell k}\varphi_{k\boldsymbol{p}-2\boldsymbol{e}_{\ell}+2\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}(t)-2\bar{A}_{\ell k}\varphi_{\ell\boldsymbol{p}-\boldsymbol{e}_{\ell}+\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}(t)]

\displaystyle+\sum_{j,k}(p_{j}-1+\delta_{jk})\bar{A}_{jk}\varphi_{\ell\boldsymbol{p}-\boldsymbol{e}_{j}+\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}(t)-\sum_{j,k}(p_{j}+1-\delta_{jk})A_{jk}\varphi_{\ell\boldsymbol{p}+\boldsymbol{e}_{j}-\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}(t)

\displaystyle+\sum_{k,r,s}(p_{r}+1-\delta_{rs}-\delta_{rk})A_{rs}A_{\ell k}\varepsilon_{\boldsymbol{p}+\boldsymbol{e}_{r}-\boldsymbol{e}_{s}-\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}(t)-\sum_{k,r,s}(p_{r}+1-\delta_{rs}+\delta_{rk}-2\delta_{r\ell})A_{rs}\bar{A}_{\ell k}\varepsilon_{\boldsymbol{p}+\boldsymbol{e}_{r}-\boldsymbol{e}_{s}+\boldsymbol{e}_{k}-2\boldsymbol{e}_{\ell}}(t)

\displaystyle-\sum_{k,r,s}(p_{r}-1+\delta_{rs}-\delta_{rk})\bar{A}_{rs}A_{\ell k}\varepsilon_{\boldsymbol{p}-\boldsymbol{e}_{r}+\boldsymbol{e}_{s}-\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}(t)-\sum_{k,r,s}(p_{r}-1+\delta_{rs}+\delta_{rk}-2\delta_{r\ell})\bar{A}_{rs}\bar{A}_{\ell k}\varepsilon_{\boldsymbol{p}-\boldsymbol{e}_{r}+\boldsymbol{e}_{s}+\boldsymbol{e}_{k}-2\boldsymbol{e}_{\ell}}(t)\,,

for all
\ell\in\{1,...,N\}
,
\boldsymbol{p}\in\mathbb{Z}^{N}
, which represent an infinite-dimensional differential-algebraic system of equations.

S5.4
Determining matrix and its singular vectors as symmetry generator coefficients

Let’s search for state-space symmetries, i.e., those where
\xi(t,z)=0
. In this context, the determining equations become an infinite-dimensional system of ordinary differential equations

\displaystyle\dot{\varphi}_{\ell\boldsymbol{p}}(t)=

\displaystyle\sum_{k}[A_{\ell k}\varphi_{k\boldsymbol{p}}(t)+\bar{A}_{\ell k}\varphi_{k\boldsymbol{p}-2\boldsymbol{e}_{\ell}+2\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}(t)-2\bar{A}_{\ell k}\varphi_{\ell\boldsymbol{p}-\boldsymbol{e}_{\ell}+\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}(t)]

\displaystyle+\sum_{j,k}(p_{j}-1+\delta_{jk})\bar{A}_{jk}\varphi_{\ell\boldsymbol{p}-\boldsymbol{e}_{j}+\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}(t)-\sum_{j,k}(p_{j}+1-\delta_{jk})A_{jk}\varphi_{\ell\boldsymbol{p}+\boldsymbol{e}_{j}-\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}(t)\,,

where
\ell\in\{1,...,N\}
and
\boldsymbol{p}\in\mathbb{Z}^{N}
. One notices that it
forms an infinite-dimensional
linear
system of equations and that only the coefficients related to the monomials of the same total degree are dependent over one another. We can thus treat the different total degrees separately. However, we can seek a specific symmetry generator that has a finite number of nonzero coefficients (
\varphi_{\ell\boldsymbol{p}}(t))_{\ell,\boldsymbol{p}}
.
Consider that the nonzero coefficients are such that
\boldsymbol{p}
is in a finite subset
\mathbb{P}\subset\mathbb{Z}^{N}
including
d:=N\cdot\#\mathbb{P}
coefficients.
For some ordering of
(\ell,\boldsymbol{p})
, the determining equations become an overdetermined linear system of differential-algebraic equations described by

\displaystyle\dot{\boldsymbol{\varphi}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{M}\,\boldsymbol{\varphi}\,,

(S101)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{0}
