\displaystyle=\mathcal{N}\,\boldsymbol{\varphi}\,,

(S102)

where we name
\mathcal{M}
the differential determining matrix and
\mathcal{N}
the algebraic determining matrix, as they entirely determine the possibility of having a generator of symmetry or not. As one should expect,
\mathcal{M}
and
\mathcal{N}
solely depend upon the elements of the complex weight matrix
A
. The differential determining matrix
\mathcal{M}
is a
d\times d
matrix, while
\mathcal{N}
is a
r\times d
matrix where
r
is the number of equations such that
\dot{\varphi}_{\ell\boldsymbol{p}}(t)=0
for
\boldsymbol{p}\notin\mathbb{P}
. The algebraic equations appear since there are shifts of coefficients in these determining equations that yield nonzero coefficients.

The solvability of Eqs (
S101
-
S102
) is questionable since it is generally strongly overdetermined. Yet, we know that for a set
\mathbb{P}
containing sufficient
\boldsymbol{p}
’s of total degree one, there are at least two solutions (
L_{0}
and
\mathcal{K}
). In fact, if
\dot{\boldsymbol{\varphi}}=\boldsymbol{0}
, then the following recurrence relations hold:

\displaystyle 0=

\displaystyle\sum_{k}\Big[A_{\ell k}\varphi_{k\boldsymbol{p}}+\bar{A}_{\ell k}\varphi_{k\boldsymbol{p}-2\boldsymbol{e}_{\ell}+2\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}-2\bar{A}_{\ell k}\varphi_{\ell\boldsymbol{p}-\boldsymbol{e}_{\ell}+\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{j}(p_{j}-1+\delta_{jk})\bar{A}_{jk}\varphi_{\ell\boldsymbol{p}-\boldsymbol{e}_{j}+\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}-\sum_{j}(p_{j}+1-\delta_{jk})A_{jk}\varphi_{\ell\boldsymbol{p}+\boldsymbol{e}_{j}-\boldsymbol{e}_{k}}\Big]\,.

(S103)

In matrix form,

\displaystyle D(A)\,\boldsymbol{\varphi}=\boldsymbol{0}

(S104)

where
D(A)
is a
m\times d
(
m>d
) complex rectangular matrix depending on the elements of a complex square matrix
A
with imaginary diagonal and
\boldsymbol{\varphi}
is a complex vector of dimension
d\times 1
. Under what conditions on
A
does the overdetermined system
D(A)\,\boldsymbol{\varphi}=\boldsymbol{0}
admit nontrivial solutions?
The last equation means that the nullspace of
D(A)
must have a dimension greater than or equal to one (the nullspace always contains the null vector, but if it only contains
\boldsymbol{0}
, its dimension is 0). By the rank-nullity theorem
[
57
]
,

\displaystyle\dim(\mathrm{nullspace}(D(A)))=N\cdot\#\mathbb{P}-\mathrm{rank}(D(A))\,,

and thus, in order to have a symmetry, the following inequality must be satisfied:

\displaystyle\mathrm{rank}(D(A))<N\cdot\#\mathbb{P}\,.

In other words, the multiplicity of the zero singular value for
D(A)
must be greater or equal to 1. The right singular vectors with singular value 0 are the coefficients of the time-independent symmetry generator.

S5.5
Proof of Theorem 4: Peripheral constants of motion

Using diverse matrices
A
for
N=4
and
N=5
, we performed symbolic and numerical calculations (see Ref.
[
144
]
, koopman-kuramoto/symbolic/symmetries) to obtain the associated determining matrices
D(A)
, their singular value decomposition and from the singular vectors with zero singular value, symmetry generators. In this way, we inferred a class of symmetry generators that enable the creation of new constants of motion in the Kuramoto model on graph. This subsection is devoted to the proof of Thm.
2.3
(Thm.
S5.5
) on these symmetry-generated constants of motion. To that end, we first introduce a lemma that specifies the conditions (see Fig.
S5
) under which the Kuramoto model admits such symmetry generators, along with their explicit form.

Lemma S10 (
Time evolution of peripheral oscillators in the frame of their source is a symmetry).

.

If there is a source oscillator with natural frequency
\omega_{s}
and it has outgoing edges toward
r>1
disjoint subgraphs whose vertex sets are denoted by
\mathcal{W}_{1},...,\mathcal{W}_{r}
, then the Koopman generators of the subgraphs in the rotating frame of the source,

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{\eta}=\mathcal{K}_{\eta}-i\omega_{s}L_{0}^{\eta}\,,\quad\eta\in\{1,...,r\}\,,

(S105)

are generators of Lie symmetries, where
\mathcal{K}_{\eta}=\sum_{j\in\mathcal{W}_{\eta}}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{W}_{\eta}\cup\{s\}}(A_{jk}z_{k}-\bar{A}_{jk}\bar{z}_{k}z_{j}^{2})\partial_{j}

and
L_{0}^{\eta}=\sum_{j\in\mathcal{W}_{\eta}}z_{j}\partial_{j}
.

Proof.
The existence of a source connected to disjoint subsets implies that the Koopman generator splits as

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}=\mathcal{K}_{s}+\sum_{\tau=1}^{r}\mathcal{K}_{\tau}=i\omega_{s}z_{s}\partial_{s}+\sum_{\tau=1}^{r}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{W}_{\tau}}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{W}_{\tau}\cup\{s\}}(A_{jk}z_{k}-\bar{A}_{jk}\bar{z}_{k}z_{j}^{2})\partial_{j}\,.

(S106)

Lemma
S5.1
, stated in
\boldsymbol{z}
-coordinates on the
N
-torus, implies that satisfying the commutation relations
[\mathcal{K},\mathcal{S}_{\eta}]=0
for all
\eta
is a sufficient condition for the present lemma to hold. Now, as illustrated in Fig.
S5
, each subgraph
\mathcal{W}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{W}_{r}
has a certain fraction of vertices contained in
\mathcal{R}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{R}_{r}
that receive from the source. The generator of the source
\mathcal{K}_{s}
only acts on the phase of these oscillators and it is convenient to split the generators related to the subgraphs as

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{\tau}=\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{R}_{\tau}}+\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{W}_{\tau}},\quad\tau\in\{1,...,r\}\,,

where
\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{R}_{\tau}}=\sum_{j\in\mathcal{R}_{\tau}}(A_{js}z_{s}-\bar{A}_{js}\bar{z}_{s}z_{j}^{2})\partial_{j}
and
\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{W}_{\tau}}=\sum_{j,k\in\mathcal{W}_{\tau}}(A_{jk}z_{k}-\bar{A}_{jk}\bar{z}_{k}z_{j}^{2})\partial_{j}
. Hence,

\displaystyle[\mathcal{K},\mathcal{S}_{\eta}]=[\,i\omega_{s}z_{s}\partial_{s}+\textstyle{\sum_{\tau=1}^{r}}(\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{R}_{\tau}}+\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{W}_{\tau}})\,\,,\,\,\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{R}_{\eta}}+\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{W}_{\eta}}-i\omega_{s}L_{0}^{\eta}\,]\,.

Using bilinearity and keeping only the nontrivial commutators yields

\displaystyle[\mathcal{K},\mathcal{S}_{\eta}]=i\omega_{s}[z_{s}\partial_{s},\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{R}_{\eta}}]-i\omega_{s}[\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{R}_{\eta}}+\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{W}_{\eta}},L_{0}^{\eta}]\,.

But clearly, one also finds
[\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{W}_{\eta}},L_{0}^{\eta}]=0
with the commutation relations of Lem.
S5.3
(or the intuition that
L_{0}^{\eta}
is the dilatation symmetry generator for
\mathcal{W}_{\eta}
and thus commutes with
\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{W}_{\eta}}
) and
[\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{R}_{\eta}},L_{0}^{\eta}]=\sum_{k\in\mathcal{R}_{\eta}}[\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{R}_{\eta}},z_{k}\partial_{k}]
. Hence,

\displaystyle[\mathcal{K},\mathcal{S}_{\eta}]

\displaystyle=i\omega_{s}(\,\textstyle{\sum_{j\in\mathcal{R}_{\eta}}}[z_{s}\partial_{s}\,,\,(A_{js}z_{s}-\bar{A}_{js}\bar{z}_{s}z_{j}^{2})\partial_{j}]-\textstyle{\sum_{j,k\in\mathcal{R}_{\eta}}}[(A_{js}z_{s}-\bar{A}_{js}\bar{z}_{s}z_{j}^{2})\partial_{j},z_{k}\partial_{k}]\,)\,.

On the one hand, the first term is

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\mathcal{R}_{\eta}}[z_{s}\partial_{s}\,,\,(A_{js}z_{s}-\bar{A}_{js}\bar{z}_{s}z_{j}^{2})\partial_{j}]=\sum_{j\in\mathcal{R}_{\eta}}(A_{js}z_{s}+\bar{A}_{js}\bar{z}_{s}z_{j}^{2})\partial_{j}\,.

On the other hand, the commutation relation
[\,\ell_{j}^{m},\,\ell_{k}^{n}\,]=\delta_{jk}(n-m)\,\ell_{j}^{m+n}
of Lem.
S5.3
implies that the second term is

\displaystyle\sum_{j,k\in\mathcal{R}_{\eta}}[(A_{js}z_{s}-\bar{A}_{js}\bar{z}_{s}z_{j}^{2})\partial_{j},z_{k}\partial_{k}]=\sum_{j,k\in\mathcal{R}_{\eta}}(A_{js}z_{s}[\ell_{j}^{-1},\ell_{k}^{0}]-\bar{A}_{js}\bar{z}_{s}[\ell_{j}^{1},\ell_{k}^{0}])=\sum_{j\in\mathcal{R}_{\eta}}(A_{js}z_{s}+\bar{A}_{js}\bar{z}_{s}z_{j}^{2})\partial_{j}\,.

Consequently,
[\mathcal{K},\mathcal{S}_{\eta}]=0
for all
\eta\in\{1,...,r\}
, so each
\mathcal{S}_{\eta}
is indeed a Lie symmetry generator of the Kuramoto dynamics.

Remark S31.
For
r=1
, the symmetry generator is
\mathcal{S}=\mathcal{K}-i\omega_{s}L_{0}
and hence,
\mathcal{S}
is linearly dependent on
\mathcal{K}
and
L_{0}
. This means that the symmetry generator does not enable the creation of a new constant of motion in the way we do in Thm.
2.3
of the main text.

Figure S5
:
Illustration of a graph with symmetry generators
\mathcal{S}_{1},...,\mathcal{S}_{r}
[Lem.
S5.5
]. A source
s
is connected to
r
subsets
\mathcal{R}_{1},...,\mathcal{R}_{r}
of peripheral parts
\mathcal{W}_{1},...,\mathcal{W}_{r}
. Each of these parts is related to a symmetry generator
\mathcal{S}_{1},...,\mathcal{S}_{r}
that makes the oscillators of the peripheral parts evolve in time in the frame of the source.

In the next theorem, we use the possible coexistence of the symmetry generators in the last lemma and the conserved cross-ratios (Thm.
S4.4
) to generate new functionally independent constants of motion.

Theorem S32 (Thm.
2.3
of the paper).

Consider that the Kuramoto model in Def. (
S1.1
) has a symmetry generator
\mathcal{S}_{\eta}
as defined in Eq. (
S105
) related to the subgraph
\mathcal{W}_{\eta}
and the source oscillator
s
.

A. If four vertices
a,b,c,d\in\mathcal{V}\setminus\{s\}
have

(A1) a unique incoming edge with weight
\mathcal{A}_{s}
from
s
;

(A2) identical natural frequencies
\omega
;

(A3) and one, two or three of them belong to
\mathcal{W}_{\eta}
,

then both the cross-ratio
c_{abcd}
and
\mathcal{S}_{\eta}[c_{abcd}]
are conserved and functionally independent.

B. If three vertices
u,v,w\in\mathcal{V}\setminus\{s\}
have

(B1) a unique incoming edge with weight
\mathcal{A}_{s}
from
s
;

(B2) identical natural frequencies
\omega=\omega_{s}-2\,\mathrm{Im}(\mathcal{A}_{s})
;

(B3) and one or two of them belong to
\mathcal{W}_{\eta}

then both the cross-ratio
c_{suvw}
and
\mathcal{S}_{\eta}[c_{suvw}]
are conserved and functionally independent.

Proof.
A. Condition (A1) implies that the four vertices are mutually disconnected (
A_{jk}=0
for all
j,k\in\{a,b,c,d\}
with
j\neq k
) as they can only have an incoming edge from the source. Therefore, condition (1) from Thm.
S4.4
is satisfied. Condition (A1) also highlights that the weights of these incoming edges are all equal to
\mathcal{A}_{s}
, meaning that condition 2 of Thm.
S4.4
holds. Then, condition 3 of Thm.
S4.4
is also fulfilled from condition (A2) and the fact that
A_{jk}=0
for all
j,k\in\{a,b,c,d\}
with
j\neq k
. Altogether, Thm.
S4.4
guarantees that
\mathcal{K}[c_{abcd}]=0
, that is,
c_{abcd}
is a constant of motion. Lemma
S5.5
shows that
\mathcal{S}_{\eta}=\mathcal{K}_{\eta}-i\omega_{s}L_{0}^{\eta}
is a symmetry generator and condition (A3) ensures that
\mathcal{S}_{\eta}[c_{abcd}]
is not zero. Therefore,
\mathcal{K}\mathcal{S}_{\eta}[c_{abcd}]=\mathcal{S}_{\eta}\mathcal{K}[c_{abcd}]=0
, i.e.,
\mathcal{S}_{\eta}[c_{abcd}]
is another constant of motion. Since
\mathcal{K}_{\eta}
depends on the source’s state
z_{s}
,
\mathcal{S}_{\eta}
and
\mathcal{S}_{\eta}[c_{abcd}]
also do. Therefore,
\mathcal{S}_{\eta}[c_{abcd}]
is functionally independent of
c_{abcd}
, which only depends on
z_{a},z_{b},z_{c},z_{d}
.

B. Conditions (B1) and (B2) imply that all the conditions of Thm.
S4.4
are fulfilled and hence,
c_{suvw}
is conserved. Then, condition (B3) guarantees that
\mathcal{S}_{\eta}[c_{suvw}]
is not zero and as in part A of the proof,
\mathcal{S}_{\eta}[c_{suvw}]
is conserved. If there are vertices other than
u
,
v
or
w
belonging to
\mathcal{W}_{\eta}
, then
\mathcal{S}_{\eta}[c_{suvw}]
is functionally independent of
c_{suvw}
. If only one or two vertices among
u
,
v
or
w
are in
\mathcal{W}_{\eta}
, the symmetry generator
\mathcal{S}_{\eta}
can take the 6 different forms

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\mathcal{S}_{\eta}^{x}=(i(\omega_{x}-\omega_{s})z_{x}+A_{xs}z_{s}-\bar{A}_{xs}\bar{z}_{s}z_{x}^{2})\partial_{x}&x\in\mathcal{W}_{\eta}\land x\in\{u,v,w\}\\
\mathcal{S}_{\eta}^{xy}=(i(\omega_{x}-\omega_{s})z_{x}+A_{xs}z_{s}-\bar{A}_{xs}\bar{z}_{s}z_{x}^{2})\partial_{x}+(i(\omega_{y}-\omega_{s})z_{y}+A_{ys}z_{s}-\bar{A}_{ys}\bar{z}_{s}z_{y}^{2})\partial_{y}&x,y\in\mathcal{W}_{\eta}\land x,y\in\{u,v,w\}\,,\end{cases}

where
\mathcal{S}_{\eta}^{xy}=\mathcal{S}_{\eta}^{yx}
and
x\neq y
. The constants of motion
c_{suvw}
and
\mathcal{S}_{\eta}[c_{suvw}]
are functionally independent if the rank of the Jacobian matrix

\displaystyle J=\begin{pmatrix}\frac{\partial c_{suvw}}{\partial z_{s}}&\frac{\partial c_{suvw}}{\partial z_{u}}&\frac{\partial c_{suvw}}{\partial z_{v}}&\frac{\partial c_{suvw}}{\partial z_{w}}\\
\frac{\partial\mathcal{S}_{\eta}[c_{suvw}]}{\partial z_{s}}&\frac{\partial\mathcal{S}_{\eta}[c_{suvw}]}{\partial z_{u}}&\frac{\partial\mathcal{S}_{\eta}[c_{suvw}]}{\partial z_{v}}&\frac{\partial\mathcal{S}_{\eta}[c_{suvw}]}{\partial z_{w}}\end{pmatrix}

is 2, where
\mathcal{S}_{\eta}
is either
\mathcal{S}_{\eta}^{u}
,
\mathcal{S}_{\eta}^{v}
,
\mathcal{S}_{\eta}^{w}
,
\mathcal{S}_{\eta}^{uv}
,
\mathcal{S}_{\eta}^{uw}
, or
\mathcal{S}_{\eta}^{vw}
. For the six Jacobian matrices, lengthy but straightforward calculations enable showing that their rank is 2 (see
proof_thm4_partB.wls
in Ref.
[
144
]
for symbolic calculations).

Remark S33.

A priori
, one could hope to generate new constants of motion from the class of symmetry generators in Lem.
S5.5
and monomial eigenfunctions. However, if there is a subgraph with vertex set
\mathcal{M}
supporting a monomial eigenfunction, it must be a source and there is no way to make
\mathcal{S}_{\eta}
act on only a subset of
\mathcal{M}
. More precisely,
\mathcal{M}
can only be a source (first condition of Thm.
S2.1
) to another vertex
v
that also receives from the source
s
. The vertex set for the subgraph admitting the symmetry generator
\mathcal{S}_{\eta}
is thus
\mathcal{W}_{\eta}=\{v\}\cup\mathcal{M}
. Using conditions 2,3,4 of Thm.
S2.1
,
\mathcal{S}_{\eta}[z^{\boldsymbol{\nu}}]=(i\sum_{j\in\mathcal{M}}\nu_{j}(\omega_{j}-\omega_{1}))\,z^{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
, i.e., the monomial is an eigenfunction of the symmetry,
\mathcal{S}_{\eta}[z^{\boldsymbol{\nu}}]
is functionally dependent on
z^{\boldsymbol{\nu}}
and
\mathcal{S}_{\eta}[z^{\boldsymbol{\nu}}]
is not a new constant of motion.

S5.6
Basic examples for Theorem 4

The example that helped us obtain Thm.
2.3
through the singular vectors of the determining matrix is the following one.

Example S34.

Consider a directed star of 5 nodes with weight matrix

\displaystyle A=\begin{pmatrix}i\omega_{1}/2&0&0&0&0\\
\mathcal{A}_{1}&i\omega/2&0&0&0\\
\mathcal{A}_{1}&0&i\omega/2&0&0\\
\mathcal{A}_{1}&0&0&i\omega/2&0\\
\mathcal{A}_{1}&0&0&0&i\omega/2\end{pmatrix}\,,

where
\mathcal{A}_{1}
is any complex number,
\omega\in\mathbb{R}
and we assume for now that
\omega_{1}\neq\omega+2\,\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{1})
. The Koopman generator of the dynamics is
\mathcal{K}=i\omega_{1}z_{1}\partial_{1}+\mathcal{K}_{2}+\mathcal{K}_{3}+\mathcal{K}_{4}+\mathcal{K}_{5}
, where
\mathcal{K}_{\eta}=(i\omega z_{\eta}+\mathcal{A}_{1}z_{1}-\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\bar{z}_{1}z_{\eta}^{2})\,\partial_{\eta}
for
\eta\in\{2,3,4,5\}
. The most straightforward approach is to write the solution
z_{1}(t)=z_{1}(0)e^{i\omega_{1}t}
and then substitute it in the four independent equations for
z_{2}
to
z_{5}
. From there, the solutions for
z_{2}(t)
to
z_{5}(t)
are found by quadrature. Yet, using the results of the paper leads to conserved observables and we can avoid computing the latter quadratures. Indeed, Thm.
S4.4
readily guarantees that there is one conserved cross-ratio

\displaystyle C_{1}(\boldsymbol{z}):=c_{2345}(\boldsymbol{z})=\frac{(z_{4}-z_{2})(z_{5}-z_{3})}{(z_{4}-z_{3})(z_{5}-z_{2})}

and four symmetries
\mathcal{S}_{\eta}=\mathcal{K}_{\eta}-i\omega_{1}z_{\eta}\partial_{\eta}
. Using the derivatives of cross-ratios computed in Eq. (
S58
), we thus find the four constants of motion

\displaystyle C_{2}(\boldsymbol{z})

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{S}_{2}[C_{1}(\boldsymbol{z})]=[i(\omega-\omega_{1})z_{2}+\mathcal{A}_{1}z_{1}-\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\bar{z}_{1}z_{2}^{2}]\frac{(z_{5}-z_{3})(z_{4}-z_{5})}{(z_{4}-z_{3})(z_{5}-z_{2})^{2}}

\displaystyle C_{3}(\boldsymbol{z})

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{S}_{3}[C_{1}(\boldsymbol{z})]=[i(\omega-\omega_{1})z_{3}+\mathcal{A}_{1}z_{1}-\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\bar{z}_{1}z_{3}^{2}]\frac{(z_{4}-z_{2})(z_{5}-z_{4})}{(z_{4}-z_{3})^{2}(z_{5}-z_{2})}

\displaystyle C_{4}(\boldsymbol{z})

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{S}_{4}[C_{1}(\boldsymbol{z})]=[i(\omega-\omega_{1})z_{4}+\mathcal{A}_{1}z_{1}-\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\bar{z}_{1}z_{4}^{2}]\frac{(z_{2}-z_{3})(z_{5}-z_{3})}{(z_{4}-z_{3})^{2}(z_{5}-z_{2})}

\displaystyle C_{5}(\boldsymbol{z})

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{S}_{5}[C_{1}(\boldsymbol{z})]=[i(\omega-\omega_{1})z_{5}+\mathcal{A}_{1}z_{1}-\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\bar{z}_{1}z_{5}^{2}]\frac{(z_{4}-z_{2})(z_{3}-z_{2})}{(z_{4}-z_{3})(z_{5}-z_{2})^{2}}\,,

which can be verified analytically with
\mathcal{K}[C_{\eta}]=0
or with symbolic calculations. Note also that
C_{2}+C_{3}+C_{4}+C_{5}=0
, meaning that there is at least one functional dependency. In fact, it is easily verified symbolically that the rank of the Jacobian matrix of
(C_{1},C_{2},...,C_{5})
is 3, so we have three functionally independent constants of motion.
Note that there is also one more. Indeed, since there is a source, we have a monomial eigenfunction
z_{1}
with eigenvalue
i\omega_{1}
and
C_{0}(t,\boldsymbol{z})=z_{1}e^{-i\omega_{1}t}
is a constant of motion. The dynamics can thus be reduced to two autonomous equations and three constants of motion (e.g.,
C_{1},C_{2},C_{3}
) or one non-autonomous equation and four constants of motion (e.g.,
C_{0},C_{1},C_{2},C_{3}
).

If, moreover,
\omega_{1}=\omega+2\,\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{1})
, then the cross-ratio

\displaystyle C_{6}(\boldsymbol{z}):=c_{1234}(\boldsymbol{z})=\frac{(z_{3}-z_{1})(z_{4}-z_{2})}{(z_{3}-z_{2})(z_{4}-z_{1})}

is also a constant of motion (
c_{1345}
and others are functionally dependent with
c_{1234},c_{2345}
as shown in subsection
S4.3
), along with

\displaystyle C_{7}(\boldsymbol{z})

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{S}_{2}[C_{6}(\boldsymbol{z})]=[i(\omega-\omega_{1})z_{2}+\mathcal{A}_{1}z_{1}-\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\bar{z}_{1}z_{2}^{2}]\frac{(z_{3}-z_{1})(z_{3}-z_{4})}{(z_{2}-z_{3})^{2}(z_{1}-z_{4})}

\displaystyle C_{8}(\boldsymbol{z})

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{S}_{3}[C_{6}(\boldsymbol{z})]=[i(\omega-\omega_{1})z_{3}+\mathcal{A}_{1}z_{1}-\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\bar{z}_{1}z_{3}^{2}]\frac{(z_{1}-z_{2})(z_{2}-z_{4})}{(z_{2}-z_{3})^{2}(z_{1}-z_{4})}

\displaystyle C_{9}(\boldsymbol{z})

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{S}_{4}[C_{6}(\boldsymbol{z})]=[i(\omega-\omega_{1})z_{4}+\mathcal{A}_{1}z_{1}-\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\bar{z}_{1}z_{4}^{2}]\frac{(z_{1}-z_{2})(z_{3}-z_{1})}{(z_{2}-z_{3})(z_{4}-z_{1})^{2}}\,.

In this case, there are 5 functionally independent constants of motion, say
C_{0},C_{1},C_{2},C_{3},C_{6}
. This completely integrates the system without having to perform quadratures. See also the symbolic calculations for this example in the Mathematica script
example_cte_mvt_from_symmetry_1.wls
for symbolic validation and Fig.
S6
for numerical validation.

Figure S6
:
Basic example with coexisting conserved quantities of different types.
The figure is related to Example
S5.6
. We evaluate the constants of motion at the phases for each time point to verify their conservation. The initial conditions
\boldsymbol{\theta}(0)\approx(4.51756368\,\,3.85865453\,\,2.66025984\,\,0.4049007\,\,2.44481427)^{\top}
are drawn from a uniform distribution. Parameters:
\alpha=\pi/3
,
\sigma=1
,
\mathcal{A}_{1}=(\sigma/4)\exp(-i\alpha)
,
\omega=1
,
\omega_{1}=\omega+2\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{1})
.

Remark S35.
By applying the symmetry generators to the logarithm of the cross-ratios, the form of the new constants of motion is simplified in the above example. For instance, assuming that
c_{2345}
is positive,

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{2}[\ln c_{2345}]=[i(\omega-\omega_{1})z_{2}+\mathcal{A}_{1}z_{1}-\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\bar{z}_{1}z_{2}^{2}]\frac{(z_{4}-z_{5})}{(z_{4}-z_{2})(z_{5}-z_{2})}\,.

If
\mathcal{A}_{1}=(\sigma/4)e^{-i\alpha}\in\mathbb{C}
with
\sigma\in\mathbb{R}
and
|\alpha|\leq\pi/2
, the real form for the constant of motion is

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{2}[2\ln c_{2345}]=(\omega-\omega_{1}+(\sigma/2)\sin(\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}-\alpha))\frac{\sin\left(\frac{\theta_{4}-\theta_{5}}{2}\right)}{\sin\left(\frac{\theta_{4}-\theta_{2}}{2}\right)\sin\left(\frac{\theta_{5}-\theta_{2}}{2}\right)}\,.

Additionally, if
\alpha=0
,
\omega_{1}=\omega
and the constant of motion is simplified to

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{2}[(2/\sigma)\ln c_{2345}]=\frac{C_{12}S_{12}S_{45}}{S_{42}S_{52}}\,,

where
S_{jk}:=\sin\left(\frac{\theta_{j}-\theta_{k}}{2}\right)
and
C_{jk}:=\cos\left(\frac{\theta_{j}-\theta_{k}}{2}\right)
.

The leaves of the star can be sources within arbitrary subgraphs. One of the simplest cases is presented in the next example.

Example S36.
Consider the star from the previous example, but connect vertices 2 and 3 to a sixth vertex and vertices 4 and 5 to a seventh vertex. The weight matrix is thus

\displaystyle A=\begin{pmatrix}i\omega_{1}/2&0&0&0&0&0&0\\
\mathcal{A}_{1}&i\omega/2&0&0&0&0&0\\
\mathcal{A}_{1}&0&i\omega/2&0&0&0&0\\
\mathcal{A}_{1}&0&0&i\omega/2&0&0&0\\
\mathcal{A}_{1}&0&0&0&i\omega/2&0&0\\
A_{61}&A_{62}&A_{63}&0&0&i\omega_{6}/2&0\\
A_{71}&0&0&A_{74}&A_{75}&0&i\omega_{7}/2\end{pmatrix}

with
\omega_{1}\neq\omega+2\,\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{1})
and the Koopman generator is
\mathcal{K}=i\omega_{1}z_{1}\partial_{1}+\sum_{\eta=2}^{5}\mathcal{K}_{\eta}+\mathcal{K}_{6}+\mathcal{K}_{7}
, where
\mathcal{K}_{2}
to
\mathcal{K}_{5}
are defined as in the previous example and
\mathcal{K}_{6}=\sum_{k=1}^{3}(A_{6k}z_{k}-\bar{A}_{6k}\bar{z}_{k}z_{6}^{2})\partial_{6}
,
\mathcal{K}_{7}=\sum_{k\in\{1,4,5\}}(A_{7k}z_{k}-\bar{A}_{7k}\bar{z}_{k}z_{7}^{2})\partial_{7}
. The monomial
z_{1}e^{-i\omega_{1}t}
and
c_{2345}
are still conserved, but there remain only two symmetries:

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{1}=\mathcal{K}_{2}+\mathcal{K}_{3}+\mathcal{K}_{6}-i\omega_{1}\sum_{j\in\{2,3,6\}}z_{j}\partial_{j}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathcal{S}_{2}=\mathcal{K}_{4}+\mathcal{K}_{5}+\mathcal{K}_{7}-i\omega_{1}\sum_{j\in\{4,5,7\}}z_{j}\partial_{j}\,.

We find that

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{1}[c_{2345}(\boldsymbol{z})]=-\mathcal{S}_{2}[c_{2345}(\boldsymbol{z})]

\displaystyle=[2i\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{1})(z_{2}z_{3}-z_{4}z_{5})+\mathcal{A}_{1}z_{1}(z_{4}+z_{5}-z_{2}-z_{3})-\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\bar{z}_{1}(z_{4}z_{5}(z_{2}+z_{3})-z_{2}z_{3}(z_{4}+z_{5}))]\frac{(z_{2}-z_{3})(z_{4}-z_{5})}{(z_{3}-z_{4})^{2}(z_{2}-z_{5})^{2}}

is another functionally independent constant of motion (see the symbolic calculations in the Mathematica scripts
example_cte_mvt_from_symmetry_2.wls
and
example_cte_mvt_from_symmetry_3.wls
when
\omega_{1}=\omega+2\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{1})
).

S5.7
Explicit form for the symmetry transformation related to
\mathcal{S}_{\eta}

We have shown in Lemma
S5.5
that

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{\eta}=\mathcal{K}_{\eta}-i\omega_{s}L_{0}^{\eta}\,,\quad\eta\in\{1,...,r\}\,,

are symmetry generators representing the time evolution of peripheral oscillators in the frame of their source. Finding the explicit action of the symmetry
\exp(\varepsilon\mathcal{S}_{\eta})
thus implies having an explicit form for the flow of the peripheral oscillators, which is generally not available when there are more than three oscillators.

There are however special cases where we are able to find the symmetry transformation more explicitly. For instance, consider the symmetry generator

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{\eta}=\rho_{\eta}(\boldsymbol{z})L_{-1}^{\eta}+i(\Omega_{\eta}-\omega_{s})L_{0}^{\eta}-\overline{\rho_{\eta}(\boldsymbol{z})}L_{1}^{\eta}

for a group of peripheral oscillators
\mathcal{W}_{\eta}
with
\#\mathcal{W}_{\eta}\geq 3
and

\displaystyle L_{n}^{\eta}=\sum_{j\in\mathcal{W}_{\eta}}z_{j}^{n+1}\partial_{j}\,,\qquad\rho_{\eta}(\boldsymbol{z})=\sum_{k\in\mathcal{W}_{\eta}\cup\{s\}}\mathcal{A}_{\eta k}z_{k}\,,\qquad\Omega_{\eta}=\omega_{\ell_{\eta}}-2\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{\eta\ell_{\eta}})\,,

where
\ell_{\eta}
is the index of any oscillator within
\mathcal{W}_{\eta}
and
\mathcal{A}_{\eta k}\in\mathbb{C}
. Note that the generator takes this form when the vertices in
\mathcal{W}_{\eta}
with
\#\mathcal{W}_{\eta}\geq 4
satisfy the conditions from Thm.
S4.4
. Without loss of generality, we can set
\omega_{s}=0
(frame of the source) and
\theta_{s}(0)=0
(
z_{s}(0)=1
) to obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{\eta}=\rho_{\eta}(\boldsymbol{z})L_{-1}^{\eta}+i\Omega_{\eta}L_{0}^{\eta}-\overline{\rho_{\eta}(\boldsymbol{z})}L_{1}^{\eta}\,,\quad\text{with}\quad\rho_{\eta}(\boldsymbol{z})=\sum_{k\in\mathcal{W}_{\eta}}\mathcal{A}_{\eta k}z_{k}+\mathcal{A}_{s}

(S107)

From there, Watanabe-Strogatz theory (following Ref.
[
88
]
) almost tells us directly, up to minor modifications, the form of
\exp(\varepsilon\mathcal{S}_{\eta})
. Indeed, given a solution
z_{j}(t)\in\mathbb{T}
for all time
t
and
j\in\mathcal{V}
, the symmetry acts as a Möbius transformation to generate the new solution

\displaystyle\tilde{z}_{j}(t)=\exp(\varepsilon\mathcal{S}_{\eta})z_{j}(t)=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\frac{e^{i\phi_{\eta}(\varepsilon)}z_{j}(t)+Z_{\eta}(\varepsilon)}{1+e^{i\phi_{\eta}(\varepsilon)}\overline{Z_{\eta}(\varepsilon)}z_{j}(t)}\,,\quad&&j\in\mathcal{W}_{\eta}\,,\\
&\qquad\quad z_{j}(t)\,,\quad&&j\in\mathcal{V}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{\eta}\,,\end{aligned}\right.

(S108)

where
Z_{\eta}(\varepsilon)
and
\phi_{\eta}(\varepsilon)
satisfy

\displaystyle\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}Z_{\eta}}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=F_{\eta}(Z_{\eta},\phi_{\eta},t)+i\Omega_{\eta}Z_{\eta}-\overline{F_{\eta}(Z_{\eta},\phi_{\eta},t)}Z_{\eta}^{2}\,\,,

(S109)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\phi_{\eta}}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\Omega_{\eta}+i(\overline{F_{\eta}(Z_{\eta},\phi_{\eta},t)}Z_{\eta}-F_{\eta}(Z_{\eta},\phi_{\eta},t)\bar{Z}_{\eta})\,,

(S110)

\displaystyle(Z_{\eta}(0),\phi_{\eta}(0))

\displaystyle=(0,0)\,,\phantom{\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}Z_{\eta}}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\varepsilon}}

(S111)

\displaystyle F_{\eta}(Z_{\eta},\phi_{\eta},t)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{A}_{s}+\sum_{k\in\mathcal{W}_{\eta}}\mathcal{A}_{\eta k}\frac{e^{i\phi_{\eta}(\varepsilon)}z_{k}(t)+Z_{\eta}(\varepsilon)}{1+e^{i\phi_{\eta}(\varepsilon)}\overline{Z_{\eta}(\varepsilon)}z_{k}(t)}\,,

(S112)

which is an autonomous dynamics (note the derivative over
\varepsilon
and not
t
). To apply the symmetry transformation and get
\tilde{z}_{j}(t)
numerically, one proceeds as follows:

1.

Obtain a solution
z_{1}(t),...,z_{N}(t)
for
t
in a discrete time-interval
t_{0},...,t_{T-1}
by integrating the equations of the Kuramoto model satisfying the conditions to have a symmetry from Lemma
S5.5
;

2.

For
t=t_{0}
, integrate Eqs. (
S109
-
S110
) with initial conditions (
S111
) from 0 to
\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
;

3.

For
t=t_{0}
, apply the Möbius transformation such as in Eq. (
S108
) to obtain
\tilde{z}_{j}(t_{0})
for
j\in\mathcal{W}_{\eta}
while
\tilde{z}_{j}(t_{0})=z_{j}(t_{0})
for
j\in\mathcal{V}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{\eta}
;

4.

Repeat step 2 and step 3 for all
t\in\{t_{1},...,t_{T-1}\}
to obtain the new solution
\tilde{z}_{1}(t),...,\tilde{z}_{N}(t)
;

One can always verify the validity of the transformed solution by integrating the related Kuramoto model at the new initial conditions generated by the symmetry transformation, provided in step 3.

If there are more than one part of peripheral oscillators satisfying the conditions of Thm.
S4.4
(say,
r^{\prime}\leq r
parts), then the procedure is essentially the same. Indeed, in such case, one has a
r^{\prime}
-parameter abelian symmetry group containing transformations of the form
\exp(\varepsilon_{1}\mathcal{S}_{1}+...+\varepsilon_{r^{\prime}}\mathcal{S}_{r^{\prime}})=\exp(\varepsilon_{1}\mathcal{S}_{1})...\exp(\varepsilon_{r^{\prime}}\mathcal{S}_{r^{\prime}})
. Again, each transformation
\exp(\varepsilon_{\eta}\mathcal{S}_{\eta})
is a different Möbius transformation and one has to integrate the equations such as Eqs. (
S109
-
S110
) from
\varepsilon_{\eta}=0
to
\varepsilon_{\eta}=\epsilon_{\eta}\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
for all
\eta\in\{1,...,r^{\prime}\}
.

Examples where a solution is transformed to another using the above formalism are provided in Ref.
[
144
]
under the name “
symmetry_action_kooku1_one_param_step0.py
”,“
symmetry_action_kooku1_two_params.py
”, “
symmetry_action_kooku1_two_params_step1.py
” (step 2 and 3). Moreover, the scripts “
symmetry_changes_invariant_sets.py
” and “
fig3_plot_invariant_sets.py
” allow reproducing Fig. 3 (
B
,
C
,
D
).

As a final remark, taken altogether, the symmetry generators
i\,L_{0}
(global dilatation or rotation of all the oscillators),
\mathcal{K}=\sum_{j,k\in\mathcal{V}}\left(A_{jk}z_{k}-\bar{A}_{jk}\bar{z}_{k}z_{j}^{2}\right)\partial_{j}
(evolution of time-independent observables), the trivial generator
f(t)\,\mathcal{U}
along with the time evolution of peripheral oscillators in the frame of the source
\mathcal{S}_{1},...,\mathcal{S}_{r}
form an abelian Lie algebra.

S5.8
Parametrized families of conserved quantities

By considering the parameters
\Theta_{1},...,\Theta_{n}
of a dynamical system as state variables that do not evolve over time, there are other possibilities of symmetry generators. Indeed, one can always write a symmetry generator as

\displaystyle v=\xi(t,\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\Theta})\partial_{t}+\sum_{j=1}^{N}\phi_{j}(t,\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\Theta})\partial_{u_{j}}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\eta_{k}(t,\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\Theta})\partial_{\Theta_{k}}\,.

In such context, one finds the following symmetry generator of the parameter-extended Kuramoto model.

Lemma S11 (Heterogeneous translation of the phases and the phase lags is a symmetry).

.

Let
\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{N}
be real numbers. The infinitesimal generator

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}

\displaystyle=i\sum_{j=1}^{N}\alpha_{j}z_{j}\partial_{j}-\sum_{p,q=1}^{N}(\alpha_{p}-\alpha_{q})\,\partial_{\alpha_{pq}}

generates a symmetry of the parameter-extended Kuramoto model acting such as

\displaystyle e^{\varepsilon\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}f(\boldsymbol{z},A,\bar{A})=f(D_{\varepsilon}\boldsymbol{z},D_{\varepsilon}AD_{-\varepsilon},D_{-\varepsilon}\bar{A}D_{\varepsilon})\,,

where
D_{\varepsilon}=\mathrm{diag}(e^{i\varepsilon\alpha_{1}},...,e^{i\varepsilon\alpha_{N}})
. In real form,
\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}=\sum_{j=1}^{N}\alpha_{j}\partial_{\theta_{j}}-\sum_{p,q=1}^{N}(\alpha_{p}-\alpha_{q})\,\partial_{\alpha_{pq}}
and

\displaystyle e^{\varepsilon\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}g(\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\alpha})=g(\boldsymbol{\theta}+\varepsilon\boldsymbol{\alpha}\,,\,\,\alpha+\varepsilon(\boldsymbol{1}\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\top}-\boldsymbol{\alpha}\boldsymbol{1}^{\top}))\,,

where
\alpha=(\alpha_{jk})_{j,k=1}^{N}
and
\boldsymbol{\alpha}=(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{N})
.

Proof.
Using the bilinearity of the commutator yields

\displaystyle[\mathcal{K},\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}]=i\sum_{j=1}^{N}\alpha_{j}[\mathcal{K},\ell_{j}^{0}]-\sum_{p,q=1}^{N}(\alpha_{p}-\alpha_{q})\,[\mathcal{K},\partial_{\alpha_{pq}}].

(S113)

Lemma
S5.3
directly gives

\displaystyle[\mathcal{K},\ell_{j}^{0}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{N}A_{jk}z_{k}\ell_{j}^{-1}+\sum_{k=1}^{N}\bar{A}_{jk}\,\bar{z}_{k}\ell_{j}^{1}-z_{j}\sum_{k=1}^{N}A_{kj}\ell_{k}^{-1}-\bar{z}_{j}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\bar{A}_{kj}\,\ell_{k}^{1}\,,

where we recall that
A_{jk}=(1/2)(W_{jk}e^{-i\alpha_{jk}}+i\omega_{j}\delta_{jk})
for all
j,k
. Hence, the first term in Eq. (
S113
) becomes

\displaystyle i\sum_{j=1}^{N}\alpha_{j}[\mathcal{K},\ell_{j}^{0}]

\displaystyle=i\sum_{j,k=1}^{N}(\alpha_{j}-\alpha_{k})\left(A_{jk}z_{k}+\bar{A}_{jk}\,\bar{z}_{k}\,z_{j}^{2}\right)\partial_{j}=\frac{i}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j,k=1\\
j\neq k\end{subarray}}^{N}(\alpha_{j}-\alpha_{k})W_{jk}\left(e^{-i\alpha_{jk}}z_{k}+e^{i\alpha_{jk}}\,\bar{z}_{k}\,z_{j}^{2}\right)\partial_{j}

after reindexing two of the four double sums. Then, for the second term in Eq. (
S113
) and some function
\psi(\boldsymbol{z},A)
,

\displaystyle[\mathcal{K},\partial_{\alpha_{pq}}]\psi(\boldsymbol{z},A)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{K}\!\left[\partial_{\alpha_{pq}}\psi(\boldsymbol{z},A)\right]-\partial_{\alpha_{pq}}\Big[\sum_{j,k=1}^{N}\left(A_{jk}z_{k}-\bar{A}_{jk}\,\bar{z}_{k}\,z_{j}^{2}\right)\partial_{j}\psi(\boldsymbol{z},A)\Big]

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j,k=1}^{N}\partial_{\alpha_{pq}}\left((W_{jk}e^{-i\alpha_{jk}}+i\omega_{j}\delta_{jk})z_{k}-(W_{jk}e^{i\alpha_{jk}}-i\omega_{j}\delta_{jk})\bar{z}_{k}\,z_{j}^{2}\right)\partial_{j}\psi(\boldsymbol{z},A)

\displaystyle=\frac{i}{2}W_{pq}\left(e^{-i\alpha_{pq}}z_{q}+e^{i\alpha_{pq}}\,\bar{z}_{q}\,z_{p}^{2}\right)\partial_{p}\psi(\boldsymbol{z},A)\phantom{\sum_{j,k=1}^{N}}

and

\displaystyle\sum_{p,q=1}^{N}(\alpha_{p}-\alpha_{q})\,[\mathcal{K},\partial_{\alpha_{pq}}]=\frac{i}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p,q=1\\
p\neq q\end{subarray}}^{N}(\alpha_{p}-\alpha_{q})W_{pq}\left(e^{-i\alpha_{pq}}z_{q}+e^{i\alpha_{pq}}\,\bar{z}_{q}\,z_{p}^{2}\right)\partial_{p}\,.

Altogether, one gets
[\mathcal{K},\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}]=0
which means that, by Lemma
S5.1
,
\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}
generates a Lie symmetry. Let
\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}=iL_{0}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}+T^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}
such that

\displaystyle L_{0}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}:=\sum_{j=1}^{N}\alpha_{j}z_{j}\partial_{j},\qquad T^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}:=-\sum_{p,q=1}^{N}(\alpha_{p}-\alpha_{q})\,\partial_{\alpha_{pq}}\,.

The flow generated by the operators
iL_{0}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}
and
T^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}
is respectively determined by the differential equations

\displaystyle\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\tilde{z}_{j}(\varepsilon)}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\varepsilon}=i\alpha_{j}\tilde{z}_{j}(\varepsilon)\,,\qquad\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\tilde{\alpha}_{jk}(\varepsilon)}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\varepsilon}=-(\alpha_{j}-\alpha_{k})

with
\tilde{z}_{j}(0)=z_{j}
and
\tilde{\alpha}_{jk}(0)=\alpha_{jk}
for all
j,k\in\{1,...,N\}
. The solutions are respectively given by

\displaystyle\tilde{z}_{j}(\varepsilon)=e^{i\varepsilon\alpha_{j}}z_{j},\qquad\tilde{\alpha}_{jk}(\varepsilon)=-(\alpha_{j}-\alpha_{k})\varepsilon+\alpha_{jk}\,.

The real form is straightforward from there, noting that
\theta_{j}+\varepsilon\alpha_{j}
for all
j
and that the transformation of the phase lags in matrix form is
\alpha+\varepsilon(\boldsymbol{1}\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\top}-\boldsymbol{\alpha}\boldsymbol{1}^{\top})
. Moreover, one finds

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(W_{jk}e^{-i\tilde{\alpha}_{jk}(\varepsilon)}+i\omega_{j}\delta_{jk})=\frac{1}{2}(W_{jk}e^{i\varepsilon(\alpha_{j}-\alpha_{k})}e^{-i\alpha_{jk}}+i\omega_{j}\delta_{jk})\,,

or in matrix form,
\tilde{A}_{\varepsilon}=D_{\varepsilon}AD_{-\varepsilon}
with
D_{\varepsilon}=\mathrm{diag}(e^{i\varepsilon\alpha_{1}},...,e^{i\varepsilon\alpha_{N}})
. Similar calculations give
\tilde{\bar{A}}_{\varepsilon}=D_{-\varepsilon}\bar{A}D_{\varepsilon}
. Since
[L_{0}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}},T^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}]=0
,
e^{\varepsilon\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}=e^{i\varepsilon L_{0}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}e^{\varepsilon T^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}
and

e^{\varepsilon\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}f(z_{1},...,z_{N},A)=f(e^{i\varepsilon\alpha_{1}}z_{1},...,e^{i\varepsilon\alpha_{N}}z_{N},D_{\varepsilon}AD_{-\varepsilon},D_{-\varepsilon}\bar{A}D_{\varepsilon})=f(D_{\varepsilon}\boldsymbol{z},D_{\varepsilon}AD_{-\varepsilon},D_{-\varepsilon}\bar{A}D_{\varepsilon})\,.

The last lemma implies that we can generate families of Koopman eigenfunctions
\psi
.

1.

Monomials
. If
z^{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
is an eigenfunction of
\mathcal{K}
(Thm
1.1
), then
\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}[z^{\boldsymbol{\mu}}]=i\left(\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mu_{j}\alpha_{j}\right)z^{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
. In this case, there is no new functionally independent eigenfunction generated by the transformation.

2.

Vandermonde-ratio
. If
V_{IJ}^{\boldsymbol{\sigma}\boldsymbol{\tau}}(\boldsymbol{z}):=(\prod_{p<q\in I}(\bar{z}_{p}-\bar{z}_{q})^{\sigma_{pq}})/(\prod_{r<s\in J}(z_{r}-z_{s})^{\tau_{rs}})
is an eigenfunction of
\mathcal{K}
, then

\displaystyle e^{-\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}[V_{IJ}^{\boldsymbol{\sigma}\boldsymbol{\tau}}(\boldsymbol{z})]

\displaystyle=\frac{\prod_{p<q\in I}(e^{i\alpha_{p}}\bar{z}_{p}-e^{i\alpha_{q}}\bar{z}_{q})^{\sigma_{pq}}}{\prod_{r<s\in J}(e^{-i\alpha_{r}}z_{r}-e^{-i\alpha_{s}}z_{s})^{\tau_{rs}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\prod_{p<q\in I}e^{i\alpha_{p}\sigma_{pq}}}{\prod_{r<s\in J}e^{-i\alpha_{r}\tau_{rs}}}\left(\frac{\prod_{p<q\in I}(\bar{z}_{p}-e^{-i(\alpha_{p}-\alpha_{q})}\bar{z}_{q})^{\frac{\sigma_{pq}}{\sigma_{p^{\prime}q^{\prime}}}}}{\prod_{r<s\in J}(z_{r}-e^{i(\alpha_{r}-\alpha_{s})}z_{s})^{\frac{\tau_{rs}}{\sigma_{p^{\prime}q^{\prime}}}}}\right)^{\sigma_{p^{\prime}q^{\prime}}}

for some
p^{\prime}<q^{\prime}\in I
. Let
m:=\#I
and
n:=\#J
. Extracting the factor in the parenthesis and absorbing
\sigma_{p^{\prime}q^{\prime}}
into
\sigma_{pq}
and
\tau_{rs}
yields

\displaystyle V_{IJ}^{\boldsymbol{\sigma}\boldsymbol{\tau}\boldsymbol{\alpha}}(\boldsymbol{z}):=\frac{\prod_{p<q\in I}(\bar{z}_{p}-e^{-i(\alpha_{p}-\alpha_{q})}\bar{z}_{q})^{\sigma_{pq}}}{\prod_{r<s\in J}(z_{r}-e^{i(\alpha_{r}-\alpha_{s})}z_{s})^{\tau_{rs}}}

for all
\boldsymbol{\sigma}
,
\boldsymbol{\tau}
,
\boldsymbol{\alpha}
, a family of Koopman eigenfunctions with
{{m+1}\choose{2}}+{{n+1}\choose{2}}-3
parameters (
{{m}\choose{2}}+{{n}\choose{2}}-1
for the
\sigma
’s and
\tau
’s and
m+n-2
for the
\alpha
’s) for the Kuramoto model satisfying the same first, second and fourth conditions of Thm.
S3.1
, but the third condition becomes

\displaystyle A_{pr}=e^{i\alpha_{p}}\mathcal{A}\,T_{r}e^{-i\alpha_{r}}\,,\qquad A_{rp}=e^{i\alpha_{r}}\bar{\mathcal{A}}\,S_{p}e^{-i\alpha_{p}}

for all
p\in I
,
r\in J
and
\mathcal{A}\in\mathbb{C}
. Validations were done for
N=5
and
N=9
vertices in Mathematica notebooks called “example_N5_extendedform.nb” and “example_N9.nb” in the folder “symbolic/eigenfunctions/vandermonde_eigenfunction” in Ref.
[
144
]
.

3.

Cross-ratios
. If
c_{abcd}(\boldsymbol{z})
is a constant of motion (Thm
S4.4
), then

\displaystyle e^{-\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}[c_{abcd}(\boldsymbol{z})]=\frac{(e^{-i\alpha_{c}}z_{c}-e^{-i\alpha_{a}}z_{a})(e^{-i\alpha_{d}}z_{d}-e^{-i\alpha_{b}}z_{b})}{(e^{-i\alpha_{c}}z_{c}-e^{-i\alpha_{b}}z_{b})(e^{-i\alpha_{d}}z_{d}-e^{-i\alpha_{a}}z_{a})}=\frac{(e^{i\gamma}z_{c}-z_{a})(e^{i\delta}z_{d}-e^{i\beta}z_{b})}{(e^{i\gamma}z_{c}-e^{i\beta}z_{b})(e^{i\delta}z_{d}-z_{a})}=:c_{abcd}^{\beta\gamma\delta}(\boldsymbol{z})\,,

for all
\beta=\alpha_{a}-\alpha_{b},\gamma=\alpha_{a}-\alpha_{c},\delta=\alpha_{a}-\alpha_{d}
form a three-parameter family of constants of motion for the Kuramoto model satisfying

(a)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} e^{i\beta}A_{ba}&=e^{i\gamma}A_{ca}=e^{i\delta}A_{da}=:\mathcal{A}_{a}\,,\\
A_{ab}&=e^{i\gamma}A_{cb}=e^{i\delta}A_{db}=:\mathcal{A}_{b}\,,\end{aligned}\qquad\begin{aligned} A_{ac}&=e^{i\beta}A_{bc}=e^{i\delta}A_{dc}=:\mathcal{A}_{c}\,,\\
A_{ad}&=e^{i\beta}A_{bd}=e^{i\gamma}A_{cd}=:\mathcal{A}_{d}\,;\end{aligned}

(S114)

(b)

A_{ak}=e^{i\beta}A_{bk}=e^{i\gamma}A_{ck}=e^{i\delta}A_{dk},\quad\forall\,k\in\{1,...,N\}\setminus\{a,b,c,d\}\,;

(S115)

(c)

\displaystyle\omega_{a}-2\Imag(\mathcal{A}_{a})=\omega_{b}-2\Imag(e^{-i\beta}\mathcal{A}_{b})=\omega_{c}-2\Imag(e^{-i\gamma}\mathcal{A}_{c})=\omega_{d}-2\Imag(e^{-i\delta}\mathcal{A}_{d})\,.

(S116)

These conditions were verified for
N=5
vertices in a Mathematica notebook called “symbolic/eigenfunctions/cross_ratio_cte_motion/example_N5_extended_crossratio.nb” in Ref.
[
144
]
.

The extension for the cross-ratios can be generalized for the complex dynamics

\displaystyle\dot{Z}_{j}=f(t,\boldsymbol{Z})e^{-i\alpha_{j}}+g(t,\boldsymbol{Z})Z_{j}+h(t,\boldsymbol{Z})e^{i\alpha_{j}}Z_{j}^{2}

(S117)

where
Z_{j}(t)\in\mathbb{C}
,
\alpha_{j}\in\mathbb{R}
for all
j\in\{1,...,N\}
, and
f,g,h
are some complex-valued functions. This is a phase-shifted version of the dynamics considered recently by Cestnik and Martens
[
22
]
. Its Koopman generator is

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{fgh}=f(t,\boldsymbol{Z})\mathcal{L}_{-1}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}+g(t,\boldsymbol{Z})\mathcal{L}_{0}+h(t,\boldsymbol{Z})\mathcal{L}_{1}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\,

where
\mathcal{L}_{-1}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}=\sum_{j=1}^{N}e^{-i\alpha_{j}}\partial_{j}
,
\mathcal{L}_{0}=\sum_{j=1}^{N}Z_{j}\partial_{j}
, and
\mathcal{L}_{1}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}=\sum_{j=1}^{N}e^{i\alpha_{j}}Z_{j}^{2}\partial_{j}
with
\partial_{j}=\partial/\partial Z_{j}
. Making the change of variables
x_{j}=e^{i\alpha_{j}}Z_{j}
implies that
\mathcal{L}_{-1}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}=\sum_{j=1}^{N}\partial/\partial x_{j}
,
\mathcal{L}_{0}=\sum_{j=1}^{N}x_{j}\partial/\partial x_{j}
,
\mathcal{L}_{1}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}=\sum_{j=1}^{N}x_{j}^{2}\partial/\partial x_{j}
, and it is clear that the complex-valued cross-ratios
(x_{c}-x_{a})(x_{d}-x_{b})/((x_{c}-x_{b})(x_{d}-x_{a}))
are the joint invariants for these operators [recall Sec.
S4.2
], and thus, conserved quantities of Eq. (
S117
). In the original variables, one gets

\displaystyle C_{abcd}(\boldsymbol{Z})=\frac{(e^{i\beta_{c}}Z_{c}-Z_{a})(e^{i\beta_{d}}Z_{d}-e^{i\beta_{b}}Z_{b})}{(e^{i\beta_{c}}Z_{c}-e^{i\beta_{b}}Z_{b})(e^{i\beta_{d}}Z_{d}-Z_{a})}\in\mathbb{C}\,.

4.

WS integrals
. Consider the WS integral
C^{\text{ws}}_{p_{1}...p_{\ell}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=\prod_{u=1}^{\ell}\sin\left(\frac{\theta_{p_{u}}-\theta_{p_{u+1}}}{2}\right)
with
p_{0}=p_{\ell}
and
p_{\ell+1}=p_{1}
. In complex form, this integral is
I(\boldsymbol{z})=\prod_{u=1}^{\ell}(1-z_{p_{u}}\bar{z}_{p_{u+1}})
. Therefore,

\displaystyle I_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}(\boldsymbol{z})=e^{-\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}[I(\boldsymbol{z})]=\prod_{u=1}^{\ell}(1-e^{i(\alpha_{p_{u+1}}-\alpha_{p_{u}})}z_{p_{u}}\bar{z}_{p_{u+1}})

for all
\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathbb{R}^{\ell}
form a family of constants of motion with
\ell-1
degrees of freedom (all the differences between
\alpha
’s). Sufficient conditions to conserve the quantities of this family are stated in Lemma
S5.8
for the sake of completeness.

5.

Peripheral constants of motion
. Consider the symmetry generator
\mathcal{S}_{\eta}=\mathcal{K}_{\eta}-i\omega_{s}L_{0}^{\eta}
. The commutator
[\mathcal{K}_{\eta},\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{\alpha}}]
is zero since
\mathcal{K}_{\eta}
for all
\eta
are independent Koopman generators. It is also easily shown that
[L_{0}^{\eta},\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}]=0
. Hence,
[\mathcal{S}_{\eta},\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}]=0
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}[\mathcal{S}_{\eta}[c_{abcd}]]=\mathcal{S}_{\eta}[\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}[c_{abcd}]]=\mathcal{S}_{\eta}[c_{abcd}^{\beta\gamma\delta}]\,,

and the symmetry generator
\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}
also extends
\mathcal{S}_{\eta}[c_{abcd}]
to a family of constants of motion. Let us consider one concrete example.

Example S37.
Recall Example
S5.6
where a star of 5 vertices admitting conserved cross-ratios and peripheral constants of motion is considered. The system admitting these conserved quantities is extended to

\displaystyle A=\begin{pmatrix}i\omega_{1}/2&0&0&0&0\\
\mathcal{A}_{1}&i\omega/2&0&0&0\\
e^{-i\beta}\mathcal{A}_{1}&0&i\omega/2&0&0\\
e^{-i\gamma}\mathcal{A}_{1}&0&0&i\omega/2&0\\
e^{-i\delta}\mathcal{A}_{1}&0&0&0&i\omega/2\end{pmatrix}\,,

where
\beta=\alpha_{2}-\alpha_{3}
,
\gamma=\alpha_{2}-\alpha_{4}
,
\delta=\alpha_{2}-\alpha_{5}
. Then, the extended conserved cross-ratio is

\displaystyle e^{-\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}c_{2345}(\boldsymbol{z})=\frac{(e^{i\gamma}z_{4}-z_{2})(e^{i\delta}z_{5}-e^{i\beta}z_{3})}{(e^{i\gamma}z_{4}-e^{i\beta}z_{3})(e^{i\delta}z_{5}-z_{2})}\,,

and an example of extended peripheral constants of motion is

\displaystyle e^{-\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}\mathcal{S}_{2}[c_{2345}(\boldsymbol{z})]

\displaystyle=[i(\omega-\omega_{1})z_{2}+\mathcal{A}_{1}z_{1}-\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\bar{z}_{1}z_{2}^{2}]\frac{(e^{i\delta}z_{5}-e^{i\beta}z_{3})(e^{i\gamma}z_{4}-e^{i\delta}z_{5})}{(e^{i\gamma}z_{4}-e^{i\beta}z_{3})(e^{i\delta}z_{5}-z_{2})^{2}}\,.

A symbolic verification is provided in Ref.
[
144
]
(example_N5_star_extended.nb in the folder symbolic/eigenfunctions/peripheral).

The following lemma states sufficient conditions to conserve a WS integral in the family generated by the symmetry
\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}
. We present it for the sake of completeness : it only corresponds to a slight adaptation of the conditions provided in Ref.
[
157
]
.

Lemma S12.

Let
\mathcal{W}\subset\mathcal{V}
be a subset of vertices
\{p_{1},\ldots,p_{\ell}\}
with
2\leq\ell\leq N
and
p_{0}:=p_{\ell}
,
p_{\ell+1}:=p_{1}
. If the Kuramoto model (
1
) satisfies

1.

\omega_{p_{u}}=\omega\in\mathbb{R}
for all
u\in\{1,\ldots,\ell\}
;

2.

W_{p_{u}k}=0
for all
u\in\{1,\ldots,\ell\}
and
k\in\mathcal{V}\setminus\mathcal{W}
;

3.

W_{p_{u}p_{v}}=w\in\mathbb{R}
for all
u,v\in\{1,\ldots,\ell\}
;

4.

\alpha_{p_{u}p_{v}}=\phi_{p_{v}}-\phi_{p_{u}}-\frac{\pi}{2}
with
\phi_{p_{u}}\in\mathbb{R}
for all
u,v\in\{1,\ldots,\ell\}
,

then

\displaystyle I=\prod_{u=1}^{\ell}\sin\left(\frac{\vartheta_{p_{u}}-\vartheta_{p_{u+1}}}{2}\right)\quad\text{with}\quad\vartheta_{p_{u}}=\theta_{p_{u}}-\phi_{p_{u}}

is a constant of motion.

Proof.
The equations are arbitrary for
j\in\mathcal{V}\setminus\mathcal{W}
in Eq. (
1
). For
j\in\mathcal{W}
, applying the conditions to Eq. (
1
) yields

\displaystyle\dot{\theta}_{p_{u}}=\dot{\vartheta}_{p_{u}}=\omega+w\sum_{v=1}^{\ell}\cos(\vartheta_{p_{v}}-\vartheta_{p_{u}})\,,\qquad\forall\,u\in\{1,...,\ell\}\,,

that is, the dynamics considered in Ref.
[
157
]
. The Koopman generator is
\mathcal{K}=\sum_{u=1}^{\ell}\dot{\theta}_{p_{u}}\partial_{p_{u}}+\mathcal{K}^{\prime}
, where
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}
is the part acting on the oscillators labelled by indices in
\mathcal{V}\setminus\mathcal{W}
. Thus,
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}[I]=0
and
\mathcal{K}[I]=\sum_{u=1}^{\ell}\dot{\theta}_{p_{u}}\partial_{p_{u}}I
. From there, the argument of Watanabe and Strogatz
[
157
, Appendix B.2]
applies directly and yields
\mathcal{K}[I]=0
.

Remark S38.
Following the subsection
S4.7
, the dynamics and the first condition of the last lemma is directly generalized by replacing
\omega
by an arbitrary function
\beta
of time and
\boldsymbol{z}
. This is because the WS function is an invariant of
L_{0}
. Note also that the diagonal elements of
W
in the third condition could be chosen to be zero without loss of generality.

Remark S39.
In complex form the constant of motion takes the form

\displaystyle\psi=\prod_{r=1}^{\ell}\left(1-x_{r}\bar{x}_{r+1}\right)=(-2i)^{\ell}I,

where
x_{r}=e^{-i\phi_{p_{r}}}z_{p_{r}}=e^{i(\theta_{p_{r}}-\phi_{p_{r}})}=:e^{i\vartheta_{p_{r}}}
. Indeed, the latter relation is obtained by noting that

\displaystyle\psi

\displaystyle=\prod_{r=1}^{\ell}\left(1-e^{i(\vartheta_{p_{r}}-\vartheta_{p_{r+1}})}\right)=(-1)^{\ell}\prod_{s=1}^{\ell}e^{i(\vartheta_{p_{s}}-\vartheta_{p_{s+1}})/2}\,\prod_{r=1}^{\ell}\left(e^{i(\vartheta_{p_{r}}-\vartheta_{p_{r+1}})/2}-e^{-i(\vartheta_{p_{r}}-\vartheta_{p_{r+1}})/2}\right),

where
\prod_{s=1}^{\ell}e^{i(\vartheta_{p_{s}}-\vartheta_{p_{s+1}})/2}=1
because of the periodic condition.
